Constitutional Article - Part 1 & Part 2

  • Part 1 (Art 1 - Art 4) - The Union & Its Territory

    • Questions Answered by Part 1 are ?
      1. What is the Name of Country ?
      2. What is the Territory ?
      3. Can it Expand ?
      4. How can it Expand ?
      5. Can India Cede to a Foreign Country ?
      6. Can it be Done with or w/o Amendment ?

    • Art 1 - Name & Territory of the Union

      • Art 1(1) - India i.e Bharat Shall be a Union of States
        • Two Names for Country → India & Bharat
          1. Why Two Names ?
          2. Ancient Name - Bharat - One Opinion
          3. Modern Name - India - Second Opinion
        • Union of States v/s Federation of States
          1. Some Members Said India i.e Bharat will be Better if it was called Federation of States ( De Centralised Power)
          2. Union of States - Feels Like - Unitary form of Government (Centralised Power)
          3. Ambedkar said that india is Federal Political System - but This Federation is Merely a Union
            1. As The Division of Power in CS - Leans Towards Union
            2. For only administrative convenience we are having states
          4. Two Important Things
            1. at no point of time, any state in India would have right to secede from the india → to prevent Balkanisation
            2. if the union wants, union can unilaterally internally reorganise states
      • Art 1(2) - State & UT name will be in First Schedule
      • Art 1(3) - Territory of India Shall Comprise(3) of

        • India = States (28) + UT (8) + Other Territory acquired in Future

        • How India will Expand its Territory ?

          1. It will be based on International Laws
          2. Whether India Acquires Territory by Normalcy or by any other method

        • How is it decided that it will be either a State or UT ?

          • Factors which Decided if its a State or UT -
            1. Population of Area
              1. Large - State
              2. Less - UT
            2. Strategic Importance of that Area was made a UT
              1. Delhi
                1. Despite High Population, Its a UT
                2. if there was a State, Union would have to take Permissions
              2. Andaman Nicobar & Lakshwdeep - National Security Reasons
            3. Cultural Differences
              1. Dadar & Nagar Haveli
              2. Puducherry

        • How is a State & UT Governed ?

          State - Federal Relationship

          UT - Directly Under State Control

    • Art 2 - Power of the Parliament to - Add Territory from Outside

      • Add a Territory - Explicitly Mentioned
        • What Happens to territory coming from outside
          1. Admit into the Union - Given Status of UT
          2. Establish New states into the Union - Given Status of State
        • Who has the Power to Admit that Territory in India ?
          1. Parliament
        • Who will Decide it is a State or UT
          1. Sikkim - was Made State - 36 CAA
          2. Goa - Initial UT & then State
          3. Puducherry & Daman & Die from Portugese - Directly UT 1962

    • Art 2A - Special Case of Sikkim : 36th CAA, 1975 ( Art 371 F added)

      1. They Carried out a Special Majority under Art 368 because when Sikkim was added to India
      2. Three Changes were Made
        1. First Schedule - By Simple Majority - List of All State and UT
        2. Fourth Schedule - By Simple Majority - Rajya Sabh Seats of State & UT
        3. Art 371 F - Special Status Given - Needed CA - By Special Majority

    • Art 3 - Power of the Parliament to - Internal Reorganisation

      Reorganise Indian Territory within Existing Territory

      • Powers of Parliament to

        One Action leads to Multiple Actions Being Performed

        1. Create a New State

          1. Merge Two States
          2. Divide Two States

        2. Increase Area of State

        3. Decrease Area of State

        4. Alter boundaries of State

        5. Alter Names of States

          1. Madras - Tamil Nadu
          2. Mysore - Karnataka
          3. Orissa - Odisha

      • Examples ( Hypothetical )

        1. All 7 North Eastern States can be merged
        2. Bundelkhand
          1. Backward Part of Country = Part of MP + Part of UP
          2. From MP & UP → Make a New Bundelkhand
        3. UT added to Tamil Nadu

      • What is the Procedure followed by the Parliament in Exercising its Power under Art 3 ?

        • Introduction of Bill

          • What is a Bill ?
            1. A Bill is a Draft Proposal for Legislation under Consideration.
          • Bill is Introduced in the ?
            • Lok Sabha & Rajya Sabha

        • Diagram

        • Example : AP Reorganisation Act, 2015 && AP Reorganisation Bill, 2015

        • Procedure - Process of Constitutional Amendment Bill

          • MP will seek permission from President
            • President Refers Bill to the State Legislature with a Time Limit
              • Yes - Opinion Not Binding
              • No - Opinion Not Binding
              • Delay - Opinion Not Binding
            • President Gives Permission to MP

              • Yes

                • Process in Parliament
                  1. MP will introduce Bill in Parliament (LS/RS).
                  2. MP can introduce in either
                    1. his house (can vote in his house only)
                    2. another house (cannot vote here but can introduce)
                  3. in Lok Sabha
                    1. Debate & Pass by Simple Majority
                  4. in Rajya Sabha
                    1. Debate & Pass by Simple Majority
                  5. Goes to President
                    1. President Gives his Assent (Says Yes)
                • New State is Formed
                • Constitution is Changed

                  • What are the Changes made ?

                    • First Schedule - Details of State & UT
                      • New Telangana Added - Names & Boundary Column Created
                      • AP Details Changed - Names & Boundary Column Altered
                    • Fourth Schedule - Allocation of Seats to Rajya Sabha

                      • Earlier

                        AP : 18 Seats in RS

                      • Now

                        Telangana : 7 in RS

                        AP : 11 in RS

                  • How are the Changes made ?

                    Will it take a CA for changing the Schedule ?

                    The Act Passed by the Parliament will automatically change the Schedules

                    Hence AP Reorg Act automatically changed First & Fourth Schedule

              • No

                1. Bill Cannot be introduced in Parliament

    • Art 4 - Any Law under Art 2 & Art 3 then such a Law can be made by a Simple Act of Parliament

      1. Laws made under Art 2 & Art 3 which provides for amendment of the first & fourth schedules
      2. That Law is NOT an amendment of this Constitution under Art 368
      3. Only First & Fourth Schedules are allowed to be Amended
    • Cede a Territory - No Where Mentioned in Parliament, hence decided by Supreme Court - CAA Needed
      • Re-Berubari Union v/s UOI, 1960
        • Prelude for Reference
          • Re → Stands for Presidential Reference
          • Background
            • Smoothen Process of Boundary Dispute → Cyril Radcliffe
            • Place Called : Berubari Union → 9 Sq Miles of Land was a Part of West Bengal
            • Pakistan Claimed this Place saying that
              • Northern Part had to go to East Pakistan
              • Southern Part had to go to India
            • Pakistan Said that it has gone to India as a Whole
            • Both Pak & Ind PM Met → JLN & Feroz Khan Noon → Nehru Noon Agreement, 1958
            • Govt Thought it can be done under Art 3 ; but people said that Art 3 is only internal reorganisation
            • but diminishing area of a state by ceding it to another country thus making a Net Subtraction to the States
          • Question of Law
            • Can Parliament do it under Art 3 ?
          • Logics - Put in front of SC
            • Do Not Give - Look Into Preamble
              1. India is a Sovereign - India is a Sovereign State, why India should budge to others part
            • Can Give - Sovereignity
              1. India is a Democratic Country - Democratic hence Indian people elected leaders can do
            • SC Said
              1. No point of arguing on preamble - preamble is not an integral part of constitution
          • SC Judgement
            • India cannot cede a territory by Art 3
            • But can cede by a CAA under Art 368 - by following a special majority for a Non Federal Dispute ( No State Ratification )
        • Parliaments Action

          • Enclave

            Small Pocket of Land completely surrounded by Another Country

          • Enclave Background

            India & Bangladesh → Have Poor & Porus Borders

            Each others Enclave in one Place or Another

          • 9th CAA, 1960

            1. Unilateral Ceding of Territory

          • Nehru Noon Agreement, 1968

          • Land Boundary Agreement, 1974

            1. Indian Enclave will be to Bangladesh
            2. Bangladesh Enclave will be to India
            3. We Will Exchange all the Enclaves
            4. No Matter Profit or Loss of Net Land
            5. If India Losses these Enclaves
              1. India needs a CAA under Art 368 Non Federal Method
              2. This Ceding happened by CAA

          • 100th CAA, 2015

            1. Exchange Enclaves between India & Bangladesh
        • Ultimately First Schedule was Changed by Ceding a Territory by these CAA
      • Examples
        • 9th CAA, 1960 - North to Bangladesh by CAA
          • India Retained Southern Part under Art 2
        • 100th CAA, 2015 - Indian Enclaves given to Bangladesh by CAA
          • India Received Bangladesh Enclaves under Art 2
    • States Re-Organisation Chronology in India

      • Historical Background

      • 1947 - India & Pakistan, 1947

      • 1948 - S K Dhar Commission, 1948

        NO

        1. No Linguistic Re Organisation
        2. One Language One State - Not Possible

        YE

        1. Geographical Contiguity
        2. Financial Self Sufficiency
        3. Ease of Administration

      • 1948 - JVP Committee, 1948

        1. JLN, Vallab Bhai Patel, Sita Bhai Patta Ramaaya
        2. Rejected Linguistic Re Organisation
        1. Especially fro Telugu Speaking Community in State of Madra
        2. Protest Erupted → Violence → 56 Days of Hunger Strike → Potti Sriramamalu

      • 1953 - Andhra Pradesh, 1953

        1. AP Created
        2. Similar Demands Started coming in Other Parts of Country

      • 1953 - First Re Organisation Commission (Fazal Ali), 1953

        • Rejected Idea of - One Language One State
        • But Linguistic Re Organisation can help in Maintaining the Cultural Homogeneity of People
        • Others can be taken into Account
          • Economic & Administrative Viability
          • Linguistic * Cultural Homogenity

      • 1956 - States Re-Organisation Act, 1956 under Art 3

        • Before this India had following States
          1. Part A - British Provinces
          2. Part B - Princely States
          3. Part C - Commissioner Provinces
          4. Part D - Andaman & Nicobar
        • 14 States & 6 UT was created

      • 1960 - Maharashtra and Gujarat

        1. Maharashtra for Marathi speaking people

        2. Gujarat for Gujarati speaking people

          Gujarat 15th state of Indian Union

      • 1961 - Dadra and Nagar Haveli

        1. Portuguese ruled until 1954
        2. Portuguese administration until 1961
        3. Added to Union Territory of India by 10th CAA 1961

      • 1962 - Goa Daman and Diu

        1. Acquired from Portuguese by police action in 1961
        2. Added to Indian union territory bY 12th CAA 1962
        3. Goa was Given statehood in 1987
        4. Daman and Diu remained union territory

      • 1962 - Puducherry

        1. French gave Puducherry to India in 1954
        2. Puducherry consists of Puducherry Karaikal Maher and Yanam
        3. From 1954 to 1962 it was administered as an acquired territory
        4. In 1962 added to Indian union territory by 14th CAA

      • 1963 - Nagaland

        1. Nagaland = Naga Hills + Tuensang Area → from Assam
        2. From 1961 to 1963 it was under governor of Assam
        3. Became 16th state of India in 963

      • 1966 - Haryana Chandigarh and Himachal Pradesh

        1. Punjab bifurcated to create Haryana

      • 1987 - Goa given the status of statehood

  • Part 2 (Art 5 - Art 11) - Citizenship

    • Questions it will Answer ?
      1. How to Identify citizens of India ?
      2. Who are Citizens of India ?
      3. Two Types of Citizenship
        1. One at Commencement of Citizenship
        2. One after the Independence ?
    • Why is there a Concept of Citizenship ?
      1. State Confers all the Rights on Citizens, for Aliens it is Limited in some cases
      2. State expects duties from its Citizens and sometimes Foreigners are relieved
      3. State can differentiate between Citizen and Alien
    • Who is a Citizen ?
      1. Citizens are Members of the State and owe Allegiance to it, They enjoy all Civil & Political Rights, guaranteed by the Constitution of the State

    • Art 5 - Art 8 → Citizenship Before 26 Jan 1950

         **Which All Person can become Citizen of India BEFORE 26 Jan 1950 ?**

      Deemed to be Citizen of India by Virtue of Constitution Enforcement

      ALL PEOPLE WHO WERE RESIDENTS OF INDEPENDENT UNDIVIDED INDIA

      • Art 5 - Domicile → Domiciled in India before 26 Jan 1950
        1. Domicile → Residence + Intention
          1. Residence in India + Intention of an Individual to Make India as a Permanent Home
          2. Intention -
            1. Major - No of Years of Stay is 5 Years before 26 Jan 1950
            2. Minor - Ownership of Property
      • Art 6 - Pak to Ind → Direct Migration → (15 Aug 1947 - 26 Jan 1950)

        • Deals with Person who had Migrated to India from Pakistan

        • Pakistan to India - Majority

        • Before Commencement of Constitution if they come to India from Pakistan, they will be be made citizens of India

      • Art 7 - Permit System → Reverse Migration → (1 Mar 1947 - 26 Jan 1950)
        1. Deals with Person who migrated from India to the Now Territory of Pakistan after 1 March 1947 but had subsequently returned to India for resettlement through Permit System
        2. 1 March 1947 - When Mountbatten Plan was Finalised
        3. Migration After 1 March 1947 → Because due to Mountbatten Plan they knew, what was the Territory of India and Pakistan ; Now if they Migrate → It means they wanted to be a Part of Pakistan
        4. Before 1 March 1947 → They can come back to India before 26 Jan 1950
        5. but these people could be used as Agents of Pakistan. Hence, a Special Permit for Background Check from Indian High Council Commissioner, Pakistan was Needed

      ALL PEOPLE WHO WERE PERSON OF INDIAN ORIGIN

      • Art 8 - PIO → Person of Indian Origin applied BEFORE 26 Jan 1950
        1. Provision relating to Persons Living in Foreign Countries at the Commencement of the Constitution
        2. PS : They don’t have to be in India for the Citizenship, they can be anywhere
        • Define Person of Indian Origin (PIO) ?
          1. PIO is not a Citizen of India : A Person or whose Parents or Grand Parents are born in India as Defined in GOI Act, 1935
        • How can a PIO become Citizen of India ?
          1. Application to the Countries Embassy
          2. Provide Proof that he is a Person of Indian Origin

      • Summary of Art 5 6 7 8

    • Art 9 - No Dual Citizenship - (Before & After 26th Jan 1950)

      1. No Person Shall be a Citizen of India or be deemed to be a Citizen of India, if he has voluntarily acquired the citizenship of any foreign state
      2. Shall be Citizen → Subsequent to Commencement of the Constitution
      3. Deemed to be → under Art 5 6 7 8
      4. Voluntarily Acquired & Any Foreign State → No Dual Citizenship

    • Art 10 - Provision of Power to Parliament to Acquire & Revoke Citizenship

      1. Every Person who is or is deemed to be Citizen of India shall continue to be such citizen subject to the provision of any law made by the Parliament
        1. Concept of Citizenship is not Permanent
        2. Citizenship can be Revoked / Altered / Terminated
      2. How can it be Altered ?
        1. by Making a Law by Parliament under power of Art 11
      • Citizenship is a very Dynamic Creiteria hence Constituent Assembly gave provisions of Citizenship only until commencemnt of Constitution. After Commencement, provisions of power to parliament were made for the parliament to decide rules about citizenship by the Parliament (both acquisition and loss)

    • Art 11 → Loss (Before & After 26 Jan 1950) & Acquisition (After 26 Jan 1950) of Citizenship

      1. Parliament shall have the power to make any provisions with respect to the acquisition and termination of citizenship and all other parliaments relating to citizenship
      2. Deals with Following Action of Citizenship

        • Acquisition of Citizenship → for (After 26 Jan 1950) - PENDING

          • 1 Birth - Born in Indian Territory

            • Citizenship Act, 1955 - (26 Jan 1950 - 01 Jul 1987)
              • Concept
                1. Jus Soli - Right of Soil
              • Condition of Citizenship
                1. Born in India → (26 January 1950 - 01 July 1987)
                2. If illegal migrants comes to India and gives birth in India, child would become automatically the citizen of India
              • Issues
                1. this was very liberal and was creating problems like
                  1. taking away economic opportunity of Indian Locals
                  2. Population Issue
                  3. Security Issues
                  4. Hence Jus Soli was Restricted
            • Citizenship Act, 1986 - (1 Jul 1987 - 2nd Dec 2004)
              • Condition for Citizenship
                1. Person Should be Born in India between 1 July 1987 - 2nd December 2004
                2. Plus One Parent an Indian Citizen
              • Issues
                1. Most of the Illegal Migrants came to India and Married an Indian
            • Citizenship Act, 2003 - (from 3rd December 2004)
              • Conditions for Citizenship
                1. Born in India after 3rd December 2004
                2. One Parent an Indian citizen
                3. Other parent Not an illegal migrant
            • Exceptions
              • Convoy of a Foreign Sovereign
                1. IFS of another country marries an Indian and gives birth to a Child → Child will not be an Indian Citizen
              • Enemy Alien
                1. Who belongs to a country with which India is at War with Some Other Country → Temporary Concept
                2. Enemy Alien Marries Indian gives birth to a Child → Child will not be a Indian Citizen
                3. At the time of Birth of Child war should not be going on India
            • Special Case : Aircraft & Ships
              1. Depends on where the Ship & Aircraft is Registered → Citizenship will be that of the Nation of Registration

          • 2 Descent - Born outside Indian Territory

            Background

            1. Based on Right of Blood : Latin : Jus Sanguinis
            2. Descent Means : By Blood Line
            3. Criteria
              1. Born outside Territory of India
              2. Born to an Indian Parent ( related in blood)
              3. at time of Birth → Parents were Indian Citizens
            4. Between 26th January 1950 - before 10th December 1992
              1. Child Born Outside Territory of India
              2. Father should be Citizen of India
              3. IMPORTANT POINT : If Mother is Citizen of India ; Father is Not ; Child will not eligible for Citizenship → Criticism of Gender Injustice
            5. Between 10th December 1992 - 2nd December 2004
              1. Born Outside Territory of India
              2. Any Parent should be Citizen of India at time of Childs Birth
            6. 3rd December 2004 onwards
              1. Born Outside Territory of India
              2. Any Parent a Citizen of India
              3. Paren Should Register Birth of Child in the Nearest Indian Foreign Office
                1. This time can be relaxed by government on grounds of Genuine Reasons
                2. Nodal Authroity : Ministry of Home Affairs
              4. Now this Law was Considered to be a Gender Neutral Law

          • 3 Registration

            Three Major Categories of People who are eligible to become Citizen of India

            • Type 1 - PIO

              • PIO Card → OCI Card

                1. PIO Cardholder was Introudced by India in 1999
                2. To Recognise Contributions in FDI & Political Support
                3. PIO Cardholder Status
                  1. File Application
                  2. Pay Fees
                4. Once PIO Cardholder Status was Granted → They still had to Go for Visas and Stuff
                5. Hence some priviliges were given to them
                  1. for 15 Years No Visa was Needed
                  2. Stay for 180 Days in India without any Permission
                  3. He wil have Property Rights similar to that of Indian Citizen
                    1. in both Mobile & Immobile Properties
                    2. except for Agricultural Lands & Plantation Properties
                6. PIO were Demanding Dual Citizenships becasue
                  1. They weren’t eligible to Contest Elections
                  2. Not able to Participate in Government Exams
                7. Committee was Setup to Look into the Matter
                  1. Committee Reccomended Dual Citizenship
                  2. Parliament rejected the Reccommendation.
                8. But Government Found a Mid Way
                  1. Above PIO + Below Citizenship of India → Overseas Citizenship of India Status
                9. A PIO can Apply for OCI Status by Paying some Fees
                10. a OIC wil get
                  1. Life Long Visas
                  2. No Restrictions on Length of Stay
                  3. Have Property Rigths - Mobile & Immobile - Agricultural Lands & Plantation Properties
                11. Countries Eligible
                  1. Initially Given to only 16 Countries
                  2. All Countries in the World
                  3. Except for Pakistan
                12. PM Involved
                  1. Atal Ji - Started
                  2. Manmohan Ji - Narrowed Differences between PIO & OCI
                  3. Modi - Made both PIO & OCI Same

              • Definition of PIO

                CAA Gives the Definition as “Any Person or his Parents or Grandparent born in the Territory of India according to the Government of India Act, 1935 or any other territory acquired by India after 1947 ; and is right holding a passport of someother country”

              • Deconstruction of Definition

                1. WHO : Himself - Parents - Grandparents
                2. WHICH Territory
                  1. Territory according to GOI Act, 1935 - Undvided India
                  2. Territory acquired by India after 1947
                    1. Sikkim
                    2. Goa
                    3. Dadra and Nagar Haveli
                    4. Damn and Diu
                    5. Puducherry
                    6. Bangladesh Enclaves
                3. WHAT is current status
                  1. Holding a Passport of Other Country
                4. HOW : By Registration

              • Process

                1. This Person applies for Visa for being a Citizen of India
                  1. with the Undertaking that he will renounce citizenship of Previous Country
                2. Governemnt gives them Visa to be a Ordinary Resident
                3. Demand : 7 Years of Citizenship in India
                  1. which is NOT Continuos but can be Irregular

              • Exceptions

                1. Can Exclude Countries Like Bangladesh & Pakistan which may vary from time to time due to various Reasons
                  1. Security of Nation
                  2. Population Control in India

              • Application to ?

                • Ministry of Home Affairs
            • Type 2 - Foreigner Marrying and Indian Citizen
              1. BKG : Foreigner who is not a PIO but Marries and Indian
              2. Condition :
                1. Marries and Indian Citizen
                2. Stays in India for Period of 7 Years
            • Type 3 - OCI Status Cardholder
              • Background
                1. OCI : Overseas Citizenship of India Status
                2. Started by Government in 2003
                3. OCI are not Citizens of India but has some Privilege
                4. now this OCI Person if wants a Citizenship in India, can get Citizenship in India

          • 4 Naturalisation
            1. Any Category of Person or Any Foreigner can become an Indian Citizen provided that the Foreigner is not an illegal Migrant
            2. Defintion of Illegal Migrant :
              1. Any Foreigner who enters India w/o a Valid Document or Extends his Stays in India according the Documents is an Illegal Migrant
            3. Conditions in Third Schedule of of CAA
              1. Ordinary Resident for a Period of 12 Years
              2. undertaking to renounce the citizenship of previous country
              3. Background Verification of Good Character by Local Police
              4. Adequate Knowledge of any one Language specified in 8th Schedule (22 Languages)
              5. Bonafide Interest to Stay in India

          RELISTEN FROM HERE

          • 5 Incorporation of Territory
            1. If a New Territory becomes a Part of India, the Government of India shall specify the persons of that territory who shall be citizens of India
            2. Example - Puducherry Citizenship Order, 1962
            3. 100th CAA, 2015 - Exchange of Encalves between India and Bangladesh
            4. One cannot be Forced to take Indian Citizenship
          • 6 Special Provision as to Citizenship of Person covered under Assam Accord of 1985

            • Background

              1. Assam Accord was Signed in 1985 under Leadership of Rajeev Gandh
              2. Assam Accord was a Tripartite Agreement signed among Government of India, Government of Assam and All Assam Studnets Union (AASU) demanding identification and Deportation of Illegal Migants from Bangladesh

            • Important Details

              1. Parties
                1. Government of India
                2. Government of Assam
                3. AASU - All Assam Students Union
              2. To Prevent :
                1. Illegal Migration from Assam
                2. because of Porus Borders between India & Bangladesh
              3. Concerns with Migration
                1. Especially Economic Oppurtunities been taken away by illegal migrants
                2. Demographic Changes - some Hindu Majority distritcs became Muslim Majority Districts
              4. Main Demand
                1. Identification - Of Illegal Migrants
                2. Deportation - Back to Bangladesh

            • Important Questions of Assam Accord

              Provisions of Assam Accorded as Incorporated in Citizenship Amendment Act, 1986

              • What are the Conditions to Identify Illegal Migrants ?

                1. All Persons - Illegal Migrants
                2. before 1st Jan 1966
                  1. Can be Granted Citizenship
                  2. cuz Good Relation with East Pakistan
                  3. Leniency due to Turmoil in East Pak Government. India had Good Relations with East Pak Gov Initially
                3. 1 Jan 1966 - 25 Mar 1971
                  1. Will be Illegal Migrant
                  2. East Pak had a Elected & Stable Government
                  3. Not Deported
                  4. They will Register with Indian Authority ; From date of Registration to 10 Years in India - will be Given Citizenship
                  5. Between Registering & Citizenship - they will have all rights except for Votes
                4. after 24 March 1971
                  1. are Illegal Migrants
                  2. they will be Deported & sent to Bangladesh
                  3. Now Bangladesh has said that they will not accept these migrants as they are not Bangladeshi Citizens
                  4. Now they will be sent to Deportation Centres

              • Both India & Assam Govt had not Implemented Assam Accord

              • In 2013 in SC, case was filled stating Illegal Migrations

              • SC directed to Implement Assam Accord → NRC was done in Assam

              • in NRC it was found that 1.9 Million People were Illegal Migrants

                • 1.3 Million → Were Hindus
                • 0.6 Millions → Were Muslims
                • All These People have to be sent back to Bangladesh
                  • If Bangladesh Accepts → Deported Back
                  • If Bangladesh Doesnt → Put in Deportations Centres
          • Citizenship Amendment Act, 2019
            • The Details
              1. CAA, 2019 → It is Giving Exceptions to Some Categories of Immigrants
              2. Citizenship Amendment Act, 2019
                1. Naturalisation of Illegal Migrants
                2. of 3 Countries → Pakistan, Afghanistan & Bangladesh
                3. Exceptions to
                  1. Hindu
                  2. Sikh

                  5. Jain

                5. All these People are Eligible to become Citizens of India by Naturalisation
                6. What will these 6 Community Provide as Proofs
            • Opinion of Government
              1. All these 6 Communities are considered to be Persecuted Minority
              2. Persecuted Minority - Who are Ill Treated, Harrased, Subjected to Homicide in original country
              3. all the 3 Countries are Muslim Majority States with Official Religion as Islam
              4. Indian being a Hindu Majority Country should give hope to these Persecuted Minorities
            • Views on CAA 2019
              • Criticism of CAA, 2019
                1. Violation of Article 2014

                    1. Rohingyas from Myanmar
                    2. Tamils from Sri Lanka
                    3. Ahmediyas from Pakistan
                  3. Violation of Secular Principles
                    1. Putting Muslims is Deporation Centres
                  4. It is Against Assam Accord
                    1. Assam Accord Cut of Date : 24 March 1971
              • In Favour of CAA, 2019
                1. Government - Its an Humanitarian Law to protect Persecuted Minorities
            • Current Status
              • Matter is Sub Judice
              • But SC has not put a Stay on

        • Loss of Citizenship → for both (Deemed(before) && Acquisition (after))

          Nothing is Mentioned in the Constitution, it is decided by the Laws of Parliament.

            1. By Voluntary Act

            • Will the Child be able to restore the Citizenship on Reaching 18 Years of Age ?

            1. By Application of Law
            2. When an Indian Citizen takes CItizenship of any Other Country, his Citizenship is terminated automatically from India
            3. Here the Main Difference is that, Here Minor Childs citizenship is NOT Revoked.

              4. Outisde India
                1. Citizen of India who hasnt returned to India for a Period of 7 Years.
