    • ⭐️President - Basics

      • Articles Involved

        1. Art 52 - There shall always be a President
        2. Art 53 - Head of Union Executive
        3. Art 54 - Electoral College for Presidential Elections
        4. Art 55 - Proportional Representation
        5. Art 58 - Qualification of President
        6. Art 59 - Conditions of Presidents Office
        7. Art 61 - Impeachment
        8. Art 71 - Election Dispute of President
        9. Art 74 - Council of Ministers to Aid & Advice the President

      • Who are the Real Executive ?

        1. Real Executive is Someone Directly elected by People
        2. PM & CoM

      • Is President a Real or Nominal Executive ?

        Although He is head of Executive. he is a Nominal Head because he has to work on the Aid and Advice of CoM (Art 74)

      • Is the Aid and Advice of CoM, binding upon the President ?

        1. Until 1976 the Judiciary had to answer this Question, Many times.
        2. It was assumed by the CA, instead of a King or Queen, India will have a President & he has to play the same function performed by the Monarch in UK
        3. It wasn't explicitly mentioned in Constitution, Hence some presidents were acting in contradiction
        4. So every time in a tussle between president and govt happened - judicial intervention was needed
        5. Hence, IG brought 42nd CAA, 1976 → Word “SHALL ACT” was added.
        6. Then, 48th CAA, 1978
          1. After Emergency, 1977 Janta Party Came to Power
          2. Janta Party was ok with President being a Nominal Executive
          3. But 48th CAA
            1. Added that President can send for reconsideration *only once *
            2. if the Govt sends again, then President has to agree now

      • So Does President EVER exercise any discretionary power ?

        Yes He Sometimes Exercises, Some Situational Discretion

      • Existence of President

        1. What if President Suddenly Dies in Office
          1. VP will become President of India
          2. If VP dies
            1. CJ of India will be President
            2. If CJ dies
              1. Senior Most Judge of SC will be President

        What if Senior Most Judge Dies

        → Then Next Line of 33 Judges based on Seniority

      • Art 58 - Qualification for being the President

        • 1 Citizen of India

          Can a Naturalised Citizen be a President of India ? Yes

          Indian Constitution doesn't differentiate between a Natural Born & Naturalised Citizen - Once a Citizen he is a Citizen

          But in USA, only Natural Born Citizens are allowed

        • 2 Age : 35 Years of Age

        • 3 Qualifies for Election as a Member of House of People (Lok Sabha) - Art 84

          • 1 Citizen of India

          • 2 Not Less than 25 Years of Age

            Automatically allows for Presidents Age

          • 3 Oath / Affirmation in front of an Authorised Authority

            1. He has to file his Nomination Papers in front of Authorised Authority
            2. Authorised Authority - Decided by Election Commission
            3. He is generally a Returning Officer - Alternating
              1. Secretary General of LS - For President
              2. Secretary General of RS - For Vice President

          • 4 He/She should be an Eligible Voter

            1. He should himself be able to vote in Lok Sabha

          • 5 He/She should not hold any Office of Profit

            • 0 Office of Profit is NOT defined in the constitution

            • 1 SC Decided the Definition

            • 2 Office of Profit - Before 2006

              Any Office executive in nature, either under central govt, or state govt, or local authorities by a way of which an individual receives a salary or a remuneration or profit can be considered to be an office of profit

            • 3 Jaya Bacchan Case, 2006

              1. Jaya Bacchan was a MP in Rajya Sabha
              2. Only when they are elected as a MP, MP should not hold any office of Profit.
              3. Subsequent to being MP in RS, she was also appointed as Chairperson of Uttar Pradesh Film Development Corporation
              4. She was disqualified from being an MP of RS
              5. Jaya Bacchan said her Disqualification was wrong, but she said that she hadn't taken any salary from there.
              6. Hence, the Disqualification was Unconstitutional
              7. SC changed the definition of Office of Profit

            • 4 Office of Profit - After 2006

              Any Office Executive in nature, either under central govt, or state govt, or local authorities by a way of which an individual receives a salary or remuneration or profit is receivable in the first place

              can be considered to be an office of profit

              It is immaterial whether the individual actually receives the salary or not

      • Concept of Office of Profit

        • 0 Office of Profit is NOT defined in the constitution

        • 1 SC Decided the Definition

        • 2 Office of Profit - Before 2006

          Any Office executive in nature, either under central govt, or state govt, or local authorities by a way of which an individual receives a salary or a remuneration or profit can be considered to be an office of profit

        • 3 Jaya Bacchan Case, 2006

          1. Jaya Bacchan was a MP in Rajya Sabha
          2. Only when they are elected as a MP, MP should not hold any office of Profit.
          3. Subsequent to being MP in RS, she was also appointed as Chairperson of Uttar Pradesh Film Development Corporation
          4. She was disqualified from being an MP of RS
          5. Jaya Bacchan said her Disqualification was wrong, but she said that she hadn't taken any salary from there.
          6. Hence, the Disqualification was Unconstitutional
          7. SC changed the definition of Office of Profit

        • 4 Office of Profit - After 2006

          Any Office Executive in nature, either under central govt, or state govt, or local authorities by a way of which an individual receives a salary or remuneration or profit is receivable in the first place

          can be considered to be an office of profit

          It is immaterial whether the individual actually receives the salary or not

      • Exceptions to Office of Profit - Pres, VP, Gov, CoM (Union & State)

        1. Sitting President
        2. Sitting Vice President
        3. Sitting Governor
        4. Ministers (Union/State)

      • Art 56 - Term of Office

        1. Since the Day he assumes office → for 5 Years
        2. A President is eligible for re election as many times as he wants
        3. US Presidents only for 2 Terms
        4. Resignation
          1. to Vice President
          2. No Need to give some specific reason
        5. with Resignation comes vacancy to the President
        6. This Vacancy can only be for a period of 6 Months and until then the office has to filled with elections

      • Art 54 - Electoral College & Type of Election for President

        • Why Indirect Elections

          In a Democracy Elected Government will have the real power and once PM is elected. Having a Direct Election for President would means

          Extra Cost of Election for a Nominal Executive

          Power Tussle between PM & President

        • How is Presidential Election related to Indian being Republic ?

          No Matter What Kind of election, the election will have to be there, because India is a Republic and it needs to have a elected head of state

        • What is the Meaning of Electoral College

          Electoral College will mean all the people who will be Eligible to Vote

        • Why is it called a Indirect Election

          1. Because people elect people
          2. who then elect the president

        • Who are part of Electoral College

          • ELECTED MEMBERS of Both Houses of Parliament
            1. Lok Sabha (545)
              1. Elected
                1. 543 Members Elected - Exist
              2. Nominated
                1. 2 Members Nominated from Anglo Indian Community by President
                2. Removed by 104th CAA, 2020
            2. Rajya Sabha (238)
              1. Elected
                1. 233 Elected
              2. Members
                1. 12 Nominated - NOT Considered
          • ELECTED MEMBERS of LEGISLATIVE ASSEMBLY of States
            1. Legislative Assembly
              1. Elected
                1. No of Seats State to State
              2. Nominated 1 Member could have been nominated by the Governor - NOT Considered for Election
            2. Legislative Council - NOT CONSIDERED
          • ELECTED MEMBERS of LEGISLATIVE ASSEMBLY of NCT of Delhi && Puducherry
            1. Added by 70th CAA of 1992
            2. NCT meaning - National Capital Territory
            • Will J&K be also a part of electoral college for presidential elections

        • Who is Not Part of the Electoral College

          1. Nominated Members of Both Houses of Parliament
          2. Nominated Members of State Legislative Assemblies
          3. Elected and Nominated Members of State Legislative Council
          4. Unless & Until a CAA is done to Art 54 & 55, J&K UT is also not a Member of Electoral College

      • Art 59 - Conditions of Presidents Office

        1. Should not be a Member of Parliament or State Legislature
          1. Member of Parliament - of Lok Sabha & Rajya Sabha
          2. State Legislature - MLA & MLC
          3. An MP or MLA MLC can contest elections in being in the post but as soon as he becomes president he should resign
        2. He Should not Hold any Office of Profit
        3. He is Entitled for Official Residence
          1. He will have to live in Rashtrapati Bhawan for discharging works of president and security of president
        4. Emoluments & Allowances - Law by Parliament
          1. Law will be made by Parliaments
          2. Presidents Salary is 5 Lac Per Month
          3. Presidents Salary & Allowances cannot be varied to be his disadvantage
          4. can be varied to Advantage but not disadvantage

      • Art 55 - Manner of Election

        Concepts Needed

        • For Population - Important Point - 1971 Census

          1. Latest Census was Used until 1975

          2. Latest Census Rule was amended and changed by 42nd CAA, 1976 by IG

            They Inserted the Word : 1971 Census was to be used till the Year 2001

            & Replaced the Word : Latest Census

          3. in Year 2001, this Provision was amended by 84th CAA, 2001 - that Census of 1971 will be used until the First Census after the Year 2026

        • What is the Reason Behind this

          1. During Emergency - Idea of Sanjay Gandhi - Congress brought Family Planning in India

          2. There were instances of Forced Sterilisation in India

          3. Central Govt asked the States to Implement this Policy

          4. Southern State said : Already there is Low Population Growth in Southern State as compared to Northern State

          5. Hence, Southern States would have been in Disadvantage as the value of their MLA would have degraded

          6. Hence, 1971 Census was fixed for Levelling the Play Field

          7. Acceptable Growth Rate was thought to be achieved by 2001

            but then due to non achievement it was further amended until 2026

        • By Secret Ballot Vote (SBV)

          1. No one can know, who voted for whom
          2. The Party can give Direction but the person will vote completely on the conscience of his own

        • By Single Transferable Vote (STV)

          • What is Single Transferable Voting System

            Each and Every MP or MLA will have a Single Vote, but they can give preferences

          • How does Ballot Paper Look Like

          • FPPT System

            1. FPPT - First Past the Post System

            2. Used in Elections in Lok Sabha and Legislative Assemblies

            3. Running Race Competition :

            4. The Person has to just get only vote, no matter even if he gets one vote

          • but in Presidential Elections we have Electoral Quota

            Valid Votes - Key Word

            How does vote become Invalid

            1. No First Preference
            2. Giving Two First Preference
            3. Not Legible or some procedural Works

            Simply Said :

            Electoral Quota : > 50% + 1

            For Ex If : Total No of Valid Votes : 10 Lacs President Should Minimum Get : 5 Lacs + 1

          • Important Points for Counting Votes

            1. Giving First Preference is Compulsory
            2. EC will only count the First Preferences

          • How is Single Transferable Vote Implemented

            Now what if this Occurs

            Now 10% is ruled out

            Now Also No One is Achieving the Electoral Quota. Hence, C is ruled out

          • Summary of Presidential Elections

        By Proportional Representation (PR)

        • Value of Vote of MLA

          1. State having more population will have more say in the elections, by giving them more power

          2. Value of Vote of MLA of State is dependent on Population of State

          3. Formula for MLA

            🇮🇳 UP : Highest : 208 per Vote Sikkim : Lowest : 7 per Vote

        • Value of Vote of MP

          Total Number of Elected Members of Parliament

          LS - 543

          RS - 233

          Total = 776

        • Electoral Quota

        Anti Defection Law doesn’t apply in case of Secret Election

        🇮🇳 **Inferences - VVIMP**
        1. Value of Vote of RS & LS MP is Same
        2. Value of Vote of an MP > Value of Vote of MLA
        3. Value of Vote of a MLA is Different in Different States
        4. An MLA in Same State will have Same Value of Vote
        5. Cumulative Value of Vote of MP from Lok Sabha > Rajya Sabha

        • Summary of MLA & MP Votes Value

      • Art 71 - Election Dispute of President

        1. Only Place where Presidential Election can be challenged is in Supreme Court
        2. No Petition can be made to any High Court
        3. Supreme Court - OR Juris
          1. Original Jurisdiction
          2. Exclusive Jurisdiction
        4. Prior Acts - Will Remain Valid because
          1. To Prevent Administrative Inconvenience
          2. Anyways the President has acted upon the Aid and Advice of CoM (Art 74)
        5. Vacancy in Electoral College is NOT a Ground to challenge the Presidential Election

      • Art 61 - Procedure for Impeachment of President

        1. Grounds on which President can be Impeached is only & only

          “Violation of Constitution”

        2. Procedure

          Resolution for Removal to be Introduced in either Houses

          1. Resolution → Proposal

            Proposal Should

            1. Include the Charge
            2. Narration of Incident

          2. Proposal Approved by

            Even Before a Proposal is introduced it should be supported by 1/4th of Members of the House (in which he is trying to introduce the bill)

          3. Proposal is given to the Presiding Officer

            1. Speaker - Lok Sabha
            2. Chairman/VP - Rajya Sabha

          4. Presiding Officer will give permission only when

            1/4th Members (in which it is introduced) have seconded it

          5. Before Introduction the 14 Days Prior Notice to the President has to be given

          6. Introduced in (Either Houses)

            1. Either LS
            2. Either RS

          7. 2/3rd of total membership of the house majority is needed

          8. Resolution Passed to the Next House President has the Right to Represent (Alone or with aid of his personal council)

          9. 2/3rd of total membership in the other house is achieved

          10. President Stands Impeached

      • Vacancy of Office of President

        1. Current President Carries on

          1. Due to Expiry of the Term - To Prevent Constitutional Interregnum

        2. Vice President Takes Over

          Becomes the President for a Period of 6 Months, within 6 Months ECI has to conduct elections

          1. Death
          2. Resignation
          3. Removal by Impeachment
          4. Otherwise → Election Declared Void by SC

    • President - Advanced

      Powers & Functions of President

      • Why These Functions ?
        1. President is the Head of Union Executive
        2. All Action taken by Union Govt is taken in the Name of President
        3. Name of President Meaning (Art 74) : The President Shall act on the Aid & Advice of PM & CoM
        4. But Still, Being a Union Executive he has to perform of some function

      Major Functions of President

      • 1 Executive Powers🚀

        These are also called Administrative Powers

        Discretionary Power

        • Discretionary Power Art 78 : Right to be Informed of the Affairs of the State

          Example : Delegation led by Opposition went to president during time of Farm Laws Protests

        Normal Power

        • Normal Power Art 77 : President Makes Rules for more Convenient Transaction of Business and allocation among the ministries

          1. How Many Departments
          2. How Many Portfolio

        • Normal Powers Art 239 : Administers Union Territory Directly

          1. President is the Authority to Appoint People

          2. President can appoint an office who report to governor for functioning of UT

            UT w/o Legislative Assembly

            1. Lt Governor
            2. Commissioner
            3. Administrator

            UT with Legislative Assembly

            1. J&K
            2. Puducherry
            3. Delhi

            They have special powers and some level of autonomy but

            1. UT’s are not in Federal Relation with centre
            2. The Boss is Still President for them.

        • Normal Power : All Executive Actions are taken in the name of the President

          1. Every means Every
          2. All Simple and Major Actions

        • Normal Powers : Appointment & Removal of Various Constitutional Functionaries

          All these Constitutional Functionaries - Appointed and Removed by President

          1. CAG - Comptroller & Auditor General
          2. Election Commissioner - EIC
          3. Attorney General - AG
          4. Members of UPSC
          5. Judges of Supreme Court & High Court (Based on Recommendation of Collegium)

        • Normal Power : Power to Appoint Commissions to Investigate Conditions of SC ST OBC

          1. Appoints National Commission like
            1. NCSC - National Commission for SC
            2. NCST - National Commission for ST
            3. NCBC - National Commission for OBC

      • 2 Legislative Powers🚀

        Veto Power

        • 1 Art 111 Veto Power (Centre) : Powers to Assent Legislations & Veto Powers

          Veto for Ordinary Bills Only

          • Types of Veto Powers of President

            • Is the Word Veto Mentioned in the Constitution

              Word Veto is no where mentioned in the Constitutions

            • For what kind of bill is the Veto Done ?

              These Kinds of Vetos are only for Ordinary Bill

            • Meaning of Veto

              to refuse to give official permission for an action or plan, when other people have agreed to it

            • Example of US

              In US President has to give assent within 10 Days or it is deemed to be passed

              In India there is No Time Limit for Giving Assent

              Pocket of President of India is Much Bigger than that of US

            • Is there a Lapse Period/Time ?

              No there is no Lapse Period of Bill

            • 1 Absolute Veto - Situational Discretion

              1. Meaning : He is going to withhold the assent to the Bill

              2. Condition : This can be Done in only Two Conditions

                Situational Discretion

                Private Members Bill

                1. It is not agenda of the government
                2. govt would support it
                3. very less chances, that it might pass in lok sabha
                4. however if by luck private member passes the bill, CoM can advice the president to not give assent to the bill

                Bill is passed by the Parliament but lok sabha is dissolved

                1. Bill is passed by the Parliament
                2. But Lok Sabha is Dissolved
                  1. Because of Expiry of Term of Lok Sabha
                  2. Because of Losing Majority
                3. Concept of Care Taker Government
                  1. If LS is dissolved, because of President
                  2. as President should always be there
                  3. So there shall always be CoM to aid the President
                  4. this Govt will be called Caretaker Government

              3. Example : of Usage of Absolute Veto

                1. President R Venkat Raman (1991)
                2. Salary Allowances & Pension of Members of Parliament Amendment Bill, 1991
            • 2 Suspensive Veto - Discretionary Power

              • What is the Meaning of Suspensive Vote ?

                When the President Sends the Bill for Re Consideration

              • Why is it called Suspensive Vote?

                Because the Veto of President is suspended by the Parliament by passing a Simple Majority

              • Why is it Sent ?

                May be President is not happy with the Bill and wants it reconsidered. A breathing room for dialouge & discussion

              • How Can it be Sent ?

                1. He may send it with or w/o a Note

              • How Many Times can he send the bill for Re Consideration ?

                1. Sending the Bill for Reconsideration “on his own” Only Once is his discretionary Power
                2. Now if the Parliament again passes the Bill with Simple Majority
                3. The President has to give assent to the Bill

              • Example of usage of Suspensive Vote ?

                1. President A P J Abdul Kalam
                2. on Office of Profit Bill, 2006
                  1. a MP cant hold any office of profit
                  2. There will be some Exemptions where the MP from being Office of Profit
                  3. Kalam sent for Re Consideration
                  4. But it was again sent for assent
                  5. and now kalam sir HAD to give his assent
            • 3 Pocket Veto - No Situation

              • Meaning of Pocket Veto ?

                President does not take any action on the Bill

              • Why is there a Pocket Veto ?

                Because Constitution does not specify a time in which the President has to give assent to the Bill

              • When is it Generally Done ?

                1. Before Dissolving of Lok Sabha
                2. Before Ending of Session of Bill

              • Example of Usage of Pocket Veto ?

                1. by President Gyani Zail Singh, 1986, Post Office Interception Mail Bill, 1986 ; He Just Sat Over it
                2. Govt Term was Over, The Act was not given assent anyhow

          • Three things that can be done by the President

            • 1 Gives his Assent

              Bill is Passed

            • 2 Withhold his Assent - Is Always a Situational Veto

              1. Meaning

                Means he is communicating that he is not going to give his assent.

                As Long as he withholds his assent, Bill doesn't becomes act

              2. by Absolute Veto

            • 3 Return for Re-Consideration - Suspensive Veto

              1. Meaning

                for time being he is not giving the assent

                based on advices fom his advisors : Secretary & Other Legal Advisors

              2. by Suspensive Veto

            • 4 Sit Over It w/o Communicating - Pocket Veto

        • 2 Art 200 Veto Power (State) : Powers regarding the bill passed the state legislatures (Governor to Reserve Bill for Presidents Consideration)

          • Process of Making Law in State

            1. State Legislatures can make Laws in States
            2. Governor Gives the Assent
            3. With the Gubernatorial Assent, Bill becomes an Act

          • Powers of the Governor

            No Role of President

            • 1 Can Give Assent → Bil Becomes Act
            • Can Withhold Assent
              1. Private Member Bill
              2. Legislative Assembly is Dissolved
            • Can Send it for Re Consideration to the State Govt Once

            Role of President Involved

            • Can Reserve it for Re Consideration of President *

              • What Happens when Governor Reserves the Bill for the Assent of President

                Now Fate of Bill is in Hands of President

              • After Sending the Bill for Re Consideration, does the Governor has say in the Bill now ?

                Now Governor has no say in the assent or dissent of the bill is what soever

              • Which Bill will be Reserved for the Consideration of President by the Governor ?

                1. It is strictly on the Constitutional Discretion of Governor
                2. Exception Being for Money Bill of State

              • What can the President do when the State Bill is sent to him for Consideration ?

                • 1 Prez Give Assent to State Bill
                  1. Bill doesn't become Act
                • 2 Prez Withhold Assent to State Bill
                • 3 Prez can direct the Governor to send the Bill for Re Consideration to State Assembly
                  1. President can send the Bill to the State Legislature as many times as he wants
                  2. If the President sends the bill back for Re Consideration to State Legislature, Governor will ask the State Legislature to Re Send the Bill Back to the President within 6 Months

        Ordinance Making Power

        • 1 Art 123 : President Ordinance Making Power

          • What is ordinance ?

            Ordinance is similar to that of Law like by Legislature

          • What is ordinance making power ?

            It is the Law Making Power Entrusted in the Hands of the Executive, during extraordinary circumstances

          • Why this power was given ?

            to meet any urgent or unforeseen situation especially when the legislative body is not in session.

          • With whom advice is the ordinance made ?

            According to Art 74, President can make ordinance on the Aid & Advice of the CoM

          • According to SOP

            1. Law Making - Legislature
            2. Implementation - Executive
            3. Interpretation - Judiciary

          • Critique of ordinance making power ?

            It violates the doctrine of separation of power

          • Conditions under which Ordinance Making power can be invoked ?

            1. Recess or any one of the house of parliament not in session - Original Constitution
            2. When there is too much work in the house and govt needs an urgent Law - D C Wadhwa Case
            3. Satisfaction of President as to emergent situation - Original Constitution

          • What is the nature of ordinance ?

            It is temporary in nature, it is NOT permanent

          • What are the checks and safeguards on ordinance laws ?

            Whenever the parliament comes to session, within 6 weeks, the parliament has to pass it with simple majority

          • Is Ordinance Making a Parallel Power ?

            No, It is not. You cannot make any ordinance, when the session is in parliament.

            it can be only done when parliament is not in session

          • What is the area in which president can make ordinance ?

            The Ordinance Making Power of President is Co Terminus with Scope of Laws that can be made by parliament

          • Can ordinance be made for a Constitutional Amendment Bill ?

            No, the exception to Ordinance making power is that of non inclusion of constitutional amendment in its scope

          • Is Ordinance making power subject to Judicial Review → Chronology

            1. Ordinance Making was beyond judicial review till 1970

            • R C Copper v/s Union of India, 1970 (Bank Nationalisation Case) - Maintained that Ordinance Making Power was under Judicial Review

              1. This case made clear that what will be the nature of ordinance making power
              2. R C Cooper was the person who had major stakes in these banks
              3. Nationalisation of banks was done with Ordinance and not by normal majority
              4. He challenged it on the grounds that , 2 days before the parliament was to be in session, the ordinance was made, hence hinting Mala Fide intention
              5. SC Maintained in R C Cooper Case that

              Presidents Satisfaction on Ordinance Making is Under Judicial Review on the Grounds of Mala fide Intention

            • Govt wasn't happy that Ordinance making was under Judicial Review

            • 38th CAA, 1975 → Said that Ordinance Making Power was beyond Judicial Review

            • 44th CAA, 1978 → Ordinance making power was again brought under scope of Judicial Review

        • 2 Art 213 : Governor Ordinance Making Power

          All these 3 cases can be used in argument of ordinance making power of both president and governor

          • R C Cooper v/s UOI, 1978

            Governors Ordinance Making Power also came under Judicial Review

          • D C Wadhwa v/s State of Bihar, 1987

            Background

            1. was a professor in pune
            2. researching about ordinance making power of governor
            3. he was surprised that from 1960-80, governors of Bihar had passed more than 200 Ordinances
            4. Here in Bihar, they didn't pass it through parliament, but kept re promulgating it again and again. thus making it as a kind of law only

            SC Said

            1. Re Promulgation of Ordinance again and again is an act of violation of constitution
            2. Extra Ordinary or Exceptional Power of Ordinance Making should not be used as a Substitute of Legislature

            Criticism

            1. In Bihar, House was in Session and even then ordinance was made. Meaning that it was used as a parallel power of legislation.
            2. Because there was a lot of work in the house and they needed urgent law.
            3. Hence SC maintained that If there is too much legislative business or the time at the the disposal of the legislature is short

          • Krishna Kumar Case v/s State of Bihar, 2017

            SC Maintained

            1. Re Promulgation of Ordinance is a fraud on the constitutional power
            2. Re Promulgation is the Subversion of Democratic Legislative Process

        • Mains Question : Do you think that the ordinance making power entrusted upon the President and Governors violates the concept of separation of powers ?

        Normal Power

        • 1 Normal Power : Is an Integral Part of Parliament

          Parliament = President + LS + RS

        • 2 Normal Power : No Bill becomes an act without his assent

        • 3 Normal Power : Summoning Function of Parliament (Parliamentary Sessions)

          By President

          • Summoning Acc to Art 74 - 5 साल की शुरुआत

            Calling of the Members of Parliament to Start a Session

            The President can Summon the Session of Parliament as and where he wants.

            For Example

            In Case of Wars, Session can be called in at a different place

          • Prorogation Acc to Art 74 - 5 साल के बीच में

            Termination of Session is called Prorogation by President

          • Dissolution - 5 साल बीतने के बाद

            When Term of Lok Sabha is Over Entire House is Dissolved

          By Presiding Officer of House (Generally for Winter, Summer Kinda Session)

          • Recess → Temporary Break in Between Two Session
          • Adjournment → Temporary But a Definite Break
          • Adjournment Sine-Di →Temporary But a Indefinite Break

        • 4 Normal Power : Right to Send Message and Address the House of the Parliament

          Can send messages to the Parliament

          When a Bill is Pending for a Long Period of Time in Parliament

          considering the respect for president

          Address the Parliament

          Optional to Address

          1. When calls for Bill Pending - Addressing the House is Optional

          Compulsory to Address

          • When a New Govt is Formed → Speech made by Govt via the Mouth of President

          • Routine First Session Every Year

            During First Session of Every Year which is generally the Budget Session

            This Address can be Either

            1. To Individual Houses
            2. To Join Sitting of Houses

            This Speech is the “Report Card of Govt : On Previous Year Achievements of Govt”

          • Motion of Thanks - IMP *

            Whenever a President Addresses House, a Motion of Thanks has to be Introduced in both Lok Sabha and Rajya Sabha Independently.

            No Matter that if President has Addressed the Single House or Joint Sitting, Motion of Thanks will be Presented in

            1. Lok Sabha - Generally by CoM and PM
            2. Rajya Sabha

            Distinctively (Alag Se)

            If Motion of Thanks is not passed in

            1. LS - It is considered that the govt has lost the majority in LS and is not enjoying majority and hence the Govt has to resign

              Because PM & CoM is accountable to the Lok Sabha

            2. RS - It is merely a Embarrassment to the Govt

        • 5 Normal Power : Powers of Nomination of the Member of the House

          Original Constitution, President can Nominate

          1. Lok Sabha - Done Away with 104th CAA, 2021
            1. 2 Anglo Indians was done to ensure their adequate representation
            2. This was meant to be for next 10 Years
          2. Rajya Sabha - Still Exists
            1. 12 Members in fields of Arts, Commerce, Sports & Social Science

        • 6 Normal Power : Laying of Report before the House of the Parliament

          1. CAG
          2. Union Finance Commission
          3. NCST / NCSC / NCBC

        • 7 Normal Power : Prior Sanctions for Introduction of Certain Legislations (3/110/117 @ New State/Money/Finance)

          Why to take Permission of President ?

          Due to Importance of Some Bills, constitutions wants certain types of bills to be introduced only if recommendation is given in first place

          Examples of Such Kind of Bills of President

          Example of Bills are :

          1. Art 3 : Bill of Creation of New States

          Importance of National Unity

          1. Art 110 : Introduction of Money Bills

          Importance of Finances

          1. Art 117: Introduction of Finances Bill

          Inferences

          1. To Prevent Unnecessary Publicity of Bill
          2. To Discourage Private Member from Entering the Bills

      • 3 Judicial Powers🚀

        Art 72 Pardoning Power

        • Why this power was given to the president
          1. Judiciary is not beyond errors, hence a way if judiciary goes wrong
          2. True Idea of Justice in not merely punishment but reform of individual as well

        Powers of President are :

        • Summary of Art 72

        • 1 Commute

          Lesser Punishment with change in character

          Ex : Capital → Life Imprisonment Ex : 14 Yrs of Jail → 10 Yrs of Jail

        • 2 Remit

          reduction in duration w/o change in character

          Ex : 5 Yrs of Imprisonment → 2 Yrs of Imprisonment

        • 3 Reprieve

          delay in execution of punishment

          Ex : generally in case of capital punishment

        • 4 Respite

          short period of rest or relief under special circumstances

          Ex : special circumstances

          1. person in jail → undergoes physical disability
          2. women in jail → is expecting a baby

        • 5 Pardon

          1. It is the Highest Form of Mercy Petition
          2. Meaning
            1. completely set free
            2. absolving all consequences of punishment
          3. Example - Azam Khan
            1. Under RPA, 1951 under sec 8(1)
            2. If the punishment is of ≥ 2 years → Leads to Immediate Disqualification from Seat
            3. Then after coming out of jail he cant contest election for 6 Years

        Cases in which President can Exercise these Powers :

        • 1 Court Martial

          Military Court → Can give no of punishments including capital punishment

        • 2 In all cases where the punishment is for an offence ; to which the executive power of the union extends

          1. Here one can only go to the president if and if the laws are made only by the parliament
          2. Here punishment on any law(other than capital death) made by state

        • 3 In all cases where punishment is sentence of death

          1. In laws of state legislature and parliament
        • President cannot be approached for mercy petition, on the punishment given based on the laws made by state legislature except for capital punishment

        Supreme Court Cases

        • Maru Ram v/s Union of India, 1980

          Pardoning Power of President is under judicial review on the ground of mala fide intention in a limited manner

          like relative of CoM being pardoned etc

        • Devinder Pal Singh Case, 2013

          Power to be exercised on the aid and advice of council of ministers

        • Mahinder Singh v/s State of Punjab, 2013

          There cannot be a Oral Hearing in front of President. It can be only on the basis of Written Petition

      • 4 Emergency Powers🚀

        Background

        1. Contained in Part 18(XVIII) of the Constitution
        2. from Art 352 to Art 360

        Circumstances under which Emergency can be Proclaimed

        Based on Art 74 : Aid & Advice of CoM

        • Art 352 : To Proclaim National Emergency

          • Grounds : War, External Aggression & Armed Rebellion

            1. War - Both Sides
            2. Armed Rebellion - Within Country Maoist Activity
            3. External Aggression - Unilateral Attack from one side on Territorial Integrity & Sovereignty of India

            Original Constitution

            1. War
            2. External Aggression
            1. Internal Disturbance - This was amended by 44th CAA
            2. They replaced the Word Internal Disturbance with Armed Rebellion

          • Scope : Can be for whole Country, Part of India

          • Instances of National Emergency in India (in Totality 3 Emergencies in India)

            1. Indian China War 1962 - External Aggression - Normal
            2. (1971-1977) → India Pakistan War 19 - External Aggression 1971 - Normal
            3. (1975-1977) → Indira Gandhi Emergency, 1975 - Internal Disturbance (25 June 1975) - Controversial Emergency

          • Cases and Story Around Third Emergency

            Case: Indira Nehru Gandhi v/s Raj Narayan Singh

            • Background

              1. Raj Narayan fought against Indira Gandhi from the Same Constituency in 1975 Election

              2. RN lost. He later maintained that IG had misused resources of Govt of India for her election bias

              3. RN argued on Corrupt Practise Clause under RPA, 1951 → Saying that It was not a Free and Fair Election

              4. According to RPA, 1951 → in HC of that Constituency the case has to be filed of RP

              5. on 12th June 1975 Allahabad High Court, gave its decision → that IG was involved in Electoral Malpractice & Corrupt Practises for winning election

              6. Violation of RPA, 1951 - Found by Allahabad HC

                on Grounds of Corrupt Practise which is a ground for Disqualification,

            • Appeal in the Supreme Court

              1. IG appealed in the SC

              2. on 24th June 1975 → SC Passed an Interim Order that → Until we decide the legitimacy of IG’s election → She can continue to be the PM but w/o the right to vote

              3. cuz the question was debate is that she is MP or Not

              4. This led to Protest → Delhi Chalo Andolan → IG was quarantined in her residence

              5. They were sending direction to govt servants to not follow orders of this govt

              6. Hence on Case of Internal Disturbance Emergency was put

              7. How FR of Protestors was curbed

                Art 359 was used to curb the FR of Individuals,

            • How was the third Emergency of 1975 was Invoked, when the second emergency of 1971 made ?

              1. Here 1975 Emergency was brought in on grounds of Internal Disturbance
              2. It was politically wrong to put leaders in jail on grounds of war and aggression and suspension of FR wasn't justified on base of war and aggression
              3. On Grounds of External War and Aggression, curbing the Internal Unrest would not have been Commensurate
              4. So Third Emergency was put on grounds of Internal Disturbance, here FR could be curtailed here and all most all the opposing leaders were put in jail thus curtailing their FR

            • 39th CAA by IG

              1. Election of PM was Sub Judice in SC
              2. Crux : Pre VP PM Speaker, their action can only be challenged in front of an authority as may be appointed by an act of law
              3. IG said - Now SC cant hear her case

            • SC Struck down 39th CAA

              1. SC Declared this 39th CAA as Un-Constitutional

              2. SC Struck it down by Means of “Violation of Basic Structure”

                on grounds of Separation of Power

              3. Free and Fair Election was added to the Basic Structure of Constitution

            • Invoking of Art 396

              In Original C President can curb all Rights

              Under Art 74 - on Aid & Adv of PM - Suspended FR of Citizens

            • ADM Jabalpur Case, 1976

              1. This case tested the Viability of Invoking of Art 396
              2. SC Maintained that President can revoke all FR of all Citizens even Rt to Life and Everything
              3. Dark Day of Democracy
              4. Was Considered a Negative Case

            • SC Decision

              SC found IG Innocent

          • Concept of CoM

            1. In total there are 70 - 80 CoM
            • Cabinet Ministers
              1. Core Ministers - Cabinet Minsters - 20/25
              2. Cabinet Minsters have Independent Portfolios
            • Minister of State
              1. They may have or not have an Independent Portoflio
              2. They are in Line of Succession to be a Cabinet Minsters
            • Deputy Ministers
              1. They don’t have a Independent Charge

          • Can be Implemented in

            1. Whole Country
            2. or a Part of India i.e State

          • Procedure for Proclamation of National Emergency

            1. President can declare the National Emergency only if he is satisfied that there is a IMMINENT Danger to the Security of India

              Satisfaction → on Aid and Advice of CoM

              Imminent → No On Going Danger Needed

            2. It is not a Discretionary Power of President

            3. Grounds on which Emergency can be Invoked

              1. War
              2. External Aggression
              3. Armed Rebellion
                1. Added by 44th CAA, 1978
                2. Earlier Word was : Internal Disturbance

            4. Once President Imposed National Emergency it is not final in itself

            5. Emergency can be Imposed Directly but

            6. But Both the House of Parliament has to give approval within 1 Month → then it can continue for another 6 month

              6 Months time from the date of Proclamation of Emergency

              It was initially for 2 months 44th CAA made it 1 months

              It was initially for Indefinite Period, 44th CAA made it compulsory for a Successive Approval

            7. Parliament has to give approval with Special Majority

              It was initially Simple Majority, 44th CAA made it for a Special Majority

              Similar to that of Majority required for Amendment in Constitution

            8. Then towards the End of 6 month, another special majority mandate by the lok sabha has to be taken

          • How to End an Emergency

            1. Proclamation may be revoked by president at any time on Aid and Adv of CoM

            2. Lok Sabha can pass a Resolution to that effect by Simple Majority (only in Lok Sabha)(Not in Rajya Sabha)

              This mandate of Lok Sabha has been made binding upon president

          • Changes by 44th CAA 1978

            1. Before - Aid and Advice of CoM - Orally
            2. Now - Aid and Advice of CoM - With a Written Communication of Cabinet Specifically for Art 352
            3. Each and Every Minister of Cabinet Rank has to give it in writing to President
            4. Even if one Cabinet Minister doesn't give in writing president cant impose emergency

          • Criticism of National Emergency

            1. Was Arbitrary on SOme View
            2. Was done by one person

          • Consequences of National Emergency

            It has four fold effect on the polity of India

            Goes Against Federal Structure of India - Centre Becomes More Powerful

            • Effect on Legislative Relationship between Centre and State

              1. Introduction
                1. Federal Political Power Changes
                2. Federal Politics becomes Unitary in Characters
                3. From De Centralisation India goes to Centralisation of Power
              2. Changes are

                1. Parliament can legislate on any matter of State Lists

                2. Meaning the States Power aren’t taken away BUT

                3. Parliament will have over riding power

                  Parliament will have a concurrent power

                4. Wherever National Emergency is Imposed, State Legislature is intact but it is under the central parliament

            • Executive Relationship between Centre and State

              1. Union can give executive directions on any matters
              2. Normally
                1. CM & CoM - will carry the functions of state
                2. Final Responsibility is of CM & CoM
                3. Normally Union Cant Interfere in States Work
                4. Union and State Executive is divided
              3. Emergency
                1. CM & CoM - State Executives will continue to function
                2. Union Executives order now becomes binding on State Executive
                3. Basically State Executive become sub ordinate to Union Executive

            • Note - On Above 2

              State Legislature & Executive is Intact, but they become subordinate to Union Legislature & Executive

            • Financial Relationship between Centre and State - Re Listen from Lecture of 12 Nov

              • Tax System Normally - Normal Finance Year 41%

                1. Normally

                  Tax

                  Major Source is Tax

                  Spectrum of Collection o

                  Non Tax

                  Return from Investments

                  Borrowings

                  Collection of Fines

                  Auctioning of Natural Resources

                  Disinvestment from Public Sector

                2. Division of Tax

                  1. Centre Collects - Rs 100

                    1. Divisible Pool 50%

                      to be Shared with States

                      41% of this has to be shared with States

                      It has to be shared with all States

                    2. Non Divisible Pool 50%

                      Exclusive to Centre

                  2. State Collects - Rs 50

                    Centre can keep it all

                3. Under Art 280 of Constitution - Independent Constitutional Body appointed by President called Finance Commission

              • Centre can unilaterally alter States Share of Divisible Pool

            • Fundamental Rights between Centre and State

              1. Precondition of Art 358 & Art 359
              2. Art 358
                1. Automatically comes into effect
                2. Automatically Suspends Art 19
                3. Only in case of - War & External Aggression
              3. Art 359
                1. Not Automatic
                2. Suspension of FR done through a Presidential Order
                3. Except Art 20 & 21
                  1. Original Constitution could suspend all FR
                  2. 44th CAA made sure that Art 20 & Art 21
                4. President has to Specify which FR he is suspending
        • Art 356 : To Impose Presidents Rule / State Emergency

          • Difference between State and National Emergency

            | State Emergency | National Emergency | | --- | --- | | For a Particular State | For Country or a Part of Country | | Also Called Presidents Rule | Also Called National Emergency | | The CoM loose their Seats | The CoM Retain there position | | Leg Ass is dissolved | Leg Ass is Intact | | Power is with Parliament Can Give Power to President | Power is with the State, but the Centre can over rule it |

          • Examples / History of State Emergency in India

          • Rationale Behind this State Emergency

            At No Point of time Indian Constitution will and should not provide any scope for maintenance of an Illegitimate Government

            Ambedkar wanted This Article to be a Dead Letter in the Govt but it has been used and misused many no of time

          • Grounds : Failure of Constitutional Machinery in the State

            1. Constitutional Machinery - Not Defined in Constitution
            2. Generally Means they have failed out to perform function as mentioned in the constitution
            3. For Example :

              1. When State Govts Looses Majority in the State Legislature.

                and no other party can prove majority

                Recently in Maharashtra

              2. Hung Assembly - After Election no party is able to form Government

              3. They have majority but Govt is not able to maintain Law and Order in State.

              4. State Govt is doing something which is against constitution. For Ex Against Secular Nature of Indian Constitution

          • What can be considered a Constitutional Breakdown

            1. But this constitutional Breakdown has not been defined in the constitution
            2. this constitutional breakdown has been defined by various supreme court cases
            3. Hence Meaning of Constitutional Breakdown are
              1. Government Looses Majority
              2. Majority is preserved but the Law and Order of the State is disturbed and out of order
              3. If any state govt is carrying any activity which is anti-secular activity or politics
            4. These are just few Examples, the Interpretation of Constitutional Breakdown will be done by Supreme Court time to time

          • S R Bommai Case, 1994

            • Background

              1. Its a Land Mark Judgement wrt Imposition of President Rule in India

              2. S R Bommai was a CM of Karnataka in 1989 from Janata Party

                He is father of current CM of Karnataka Basavaraj Bommai

              3. After he became CM, few MLA from his party and other party, went to Governor, to tell that Bommai has lost majority in the state

              4. Governor sent the letter to President to impose president rule

              5. Meanwhile, Bommai went to Governor, to ask for an opportunity to prove his majority

              6. Gov didn’t listen to him, President Imposed President Rule, Bommai reached Supreme Court

            • Supreme Courts Decisions & Guidelines
                1. President Rule is Subject to Judicial Review
                2. For the First Time, SC said that Power of President for imposing State Emergency is under Judicial Review on grounds of mala fide Intention
                1. Opportunity to Prove Majority
                2. It should not be done on basis of Subjective Satisfaction of President
                3. But shall be done only on the Basis of Floor Test
                  1. w/o the floor test, if presidents rule is imposed, it would be considered unconstitutional
                  2. the floor test can only in the Legislative Assembly not in Raj Bhavan, or a bungalow, or a hotel

                1. 2 Months time for Simple Majority from House

                2. Imposition of PR by Prez is not final, it has to be put it in front of the parliament, it has to be passed within 2 months by both the houses of parliament by simple majority

                3. Very Important Point

                  Once the State Emergency is Imposed &&

                  Unless the PR is passed by simple majority by both houses of the parliament within 2 Months

                  the CoM & Leg Ass of State will be kept under Suspended Animation

                  Suspended Animation → It will not actively perform active functions but it will not be dissolved as well

                4. Why Suspended Animation →

                State Govt - Rabri Devi CM of Bihar

                National Govt - Atal Bihari Vajpayee Govt suggested imposition of PR on grounds of Law & Order but Bihar Leg Ass was with Complete Majority

                Here PR was put to houses, LS gave approval, RS didn’t

                Hence, Now Bihar Govt was put back in action, Rabri Devi became CM

                If the Govt was dissolved, Election would have been the only option

              1. If the State Govt is not Happy with PR then the Burden of Proof Lies in the Hands of Central Government
              2. SC maintained that if the PR is not passed in Parliament they will be given a Full Remedy / Complete Justice i.e by restoring the earlier govt
              3. Presidents Rule Grounds
                1. Constitutional Breakdown
                  1. Law & order
                  2. Majority Lost
                  3. Anti Secular Motives
                2. Not on grounds of Administrative Breakdowns
                  1. Not a Welfare Orientation
                  2. Lot of Corruption Charges
              4. Anti Secular Politics is Liable to Action under Art 356

          • Procedure to Impose State Emergency

            • What Happens in a Normal Condition

              1. Normally on Aid and Advice of CM & CoM, Governor Works

              2. But this process only works until and unless the government is a legitimate and constitutional government

              3. If the Particular Government is dismissed, till the time the new government is elected by election

              4. In this meantime President is given the power to administer the state

              5. President Rule is Applied when the

                President is Satisfied that there is Constitutional Breakdown in the State

            • President on Receipt of Report of Governor or Otherwise (Art 356)

              1. whenever he feels that there is a constitutional breakdown
              2. Normally Governor sends report on Constitutional Breakdown
              3. But It is not necessary for the President to wait for the report of governor
              4. It is not the Discretionary Power but on Aid & Advice of CoM

            • Approved both Houses within 2 Months

              1. By Simple Majority
              2. Leg Ass of State will be until then put under Suspended Animation
              3. Once Approved the President Rule can extend upto duration of 6 Months

            • Can be extended by 6 Months once approved for a Maximum of 3 Years. However Beyond one year it can be extended only when

              1. Before this 6 Month Ends, Another Simple Majority can extend the State Emergency to another 6 Months

              2. This can be done only for 6 times that is for a totality of 3 Months

              3. Imp Point : Upto 1 Year there is No Issue

              4. From 1 Year to 3 Years, it can be extended only under 2 conditions only

                1. There Should be National Emergency
                  1. In Entire Country
                  2. Or Any Part of Country where the State Exists
                2. && The Election Commission of India certifies that due to the National Emergency it is difficult to carry out Elections

                Note : Both Conditions have to be fulfilled

            • Revoked by the President

              By AA of CoM President can revoke the President Rule

          • Consequences of State Emergency

            • Important Point

              That Particular State looses Federal Relation with the Centre

              The Relationship between Centre and State will change only with that State and no other state is affected

              B R Ambedkar Supported this provision and wanted it to be a part of it

            • Consequence 1 - Executive Relationship

              All Executive Authority is taken up by the President

            • Consequences 2 - Legislative Relationship

              The Legislative Powers of the State shall be exercisable by or under the authority of the parliament or confer power upon the president

              1. CoM is bound to be Dismissed
              2. Legislative Assembly is put under Suspended Animation
        • Art 360 : To Proclaim Financial Emergency

          • Grounds : Threat to Financial Stability or Credit of India

            Conditions are

            1. High Fiscal Deficit

              Expenditure High Income Low

            2. Lack of Balance of Payment

            3. Inflation is High

            4. Unemployment is Very High

            5. Insufficient Foreign Exchange Reserves

              Reserve should be for the Minimum of 3 Months

          • Instances

            There has not yet been a Single Financial Emergency in India Yet

            Example of One Instance where it could have happened

            1. in 1990 Govt of India had only foreign reserve for only 15 days, after 15 days there would not have been a energy source
            2. Theres no Example as such yet
            3. There was consideration of Financial Emergency in 1990’s
            4. IMF Help was given on the Condition of Liberalisation

          • Declaration of Financial Emergency

            • The President can declare the Financial Emergency if he is SATISFIED that

              Satisfied Means → Not Necessarily the Condition has occurred but if only the President is Satisfied

              the Financial Stability & Credit of India is threatened

            • Declaration to be Approved within 2 Months by the House of Parliament

              Approval by Simple Majority

          • Consequences of Financial Emergency

              1. The Union Government may give direction the State to Observe Canons of Financial Propriety

              Centre generally doesn’t have a say so as to how the State can spend the Money

              But in Financial Emergency, Centre can direct the State on Financials

              1. Reduction in Salaries on all or any class of person serving in the State Governance
              1. Reservation of Money Bills or Financial Bills of the Consideration of the Presidents

              Centre can ask the President

              President may ask the Gov to Reserve all Bill

              Governor will have to Mandatorily Reserve the Bill

              1. Reduction in Salaries and Allowances of all classes of person serving in the Central Government including Judges of Supreme Court and High Court
        • Ambedkar's Comment on Emergency

          1. We cannot allow a unconstitutional govt

            I envisaged it to be the Dead Letter in Constitution

          2. Its been used as a Deadly Weapon now

      • 5 Financial Powers

        It is the Financial Nature of Executive and Emergency Powers of President

        The president can reduce the salaries of all government officials, including judges of the supreme court and high courts, in cases of a financial emergency.

        All money bills passed by state legislatures are submitted to the president for approval.

    Minor Functions - Extension of Executive Powers

    • 6 Military Powers

      President is the Ex Officio Head of Military of all 3 Wings - Also called the “Supreme Commander”

    • 7 Diplomatic Powers

      All Signatures are done in the Name of President

      1. International Treaty are Done
      2. Welcoming of International Guests
    • Vice President

      • Introduction to Office of Vice President

        1. There shall be an Office of Vice President

        2. Ex Officio Chairman of Council of States → Someone holds an office by virtue of holding ant other office

        3. Rajya Sabha

          1. Chairman - not elected
          2. Deputy Chairman - elected

        4. Acts as President when Office of President is Vacant due to

          1. Due to Death
          2. Resignation
          3. Removal of President
          4. Otherwise

          Not in case

          e. NOT due to Expiry of End of term of Incumbent President

        5. Example 1

          1. VP B D Jatti - Became President
          2. Fakhruddin Ali Ahmed - Died as President in February 1977

      • Election of Vice President

        1. Indirectly Elected - Not a Significant Role

        2. Electoral College - Very Less

          Members of Both Houses of Parliament (Both Nominated and Elected Members of the House)

          Very Important Note : In case of VP → Both Elected & Nominated Members are included in Electoral College

      • Qualification for being the Vice President

        • 1 Citizen of India

          Can a Naturalised Citizen be a President of India ? Yes

          Indian Constitution doesn't differentiate between a Natural Born & Naturalised Citizen - Once a Citizen he is a Citizen

          But in USA, only Natural Born Citizens are allowed

        • 2 Age : Completed 35 Years of Age

        • 3 He should eligible to contest election to Rajya Sabha (Council of States)

          • 1 Citizen of India

          • 2 Not Less than 30 Years of Age

            Automatically allows for Presidents Age

          • 3 Oath / Affirmation in front of an Authorised Authority

            1. He has to file his Nomination Papers in front of Authorised Authority
            2. Authorised Authority - Decided by Election Commission
            3. He is generally a Returning Officer - Alternating
              1. For President → Secretary General of LS
              2. For Vice President → Secretary General of RS

          • 4 He/She should be an Eligible Voter

            1. He should himself be able to vote in Lok Sabha

          • 5 He/She should not hold any office of Profit

            • 0 Office of Profit is NOT defined in the constitution

            • 1 SC Decided the Definition

            • 2 Office of Profit - Before 2006

              Any Office executive in nature, either under central govt, or state govt, or local authorities by a way of which an individual receives a salary or a remuneration or profit can be considered to be an office of profit

            • 3 Jaya Bacchan Case, 2006

              1. Jaya Bacchan was a MP in Rajya Sabha
              2. Only when they are elected as a MP, MP should not hold any office of Profit.
              3. Subsequent to being MP in RS, she was also appointed as Chairperson of Uttar Pradesh Film Development Corporation
              4. She was disqualified from being an MP of RS
              5. Jaya Bacchan said her Disqualification was wrong, but she said that she hadn't taken any salary from there.
              6. Hence, the Disqualification was Unconstitutional
              7. SC changed the definition of Office of Profit

            • 4 Office of Profit - After 2006

              Any Office Executive in nature, either under central govt, or state govt, or local authorities by a way of which an individual receives a salary or remuneration or profit is receivable in the first place

              can be considered to be an office of profit

              It is immaterial whether the individual actually receives the salary or not

          • Exceptions to Office of Profit

            1. Sitting President
            2. Sitting Vice President
            3. Sitting Governor
            4. Ministers (Union/State)

        • Term of Office of Vice President

          1. 5 Years of Tenure
          2. Resignation Given to the President
          3. Removed by Resolution passed by Effective Majority - Rajya Sabha Simple Majority - Lok Sabha (Simple Majority Art 100)
          4. For VP it can be introduced only in the Rajya Sabha

        • Note for Death of Vice President

          Vacancy to Office of Vice President to be filled as soon as possible

          ECI should conduct election as soon as possible

        • Vacancy to Office of VP - Same as President

        • Oath to be Administered by President

        • Election of Vice President can be challenged in Court of Law

          • Original & Exclusive Jurisdiction of Supreme Court

        • Prior Acts of VP will not be Invalidated

        • Election of VP cannot be invalidated merely on vacancy of electoral collage

    • Powers & Functions of Vice President
      1. His only function is to be the Ex Officio Chairman of Rajya Sabha
      2. To be the President when President Dies in Office

    • Governor

        1. Head of State Executive. He is the Constitutional head of the State

        1. He works on Aid and Advice of CoM

        2. President is Nominal Head of States

          Governor is Nominal Executive of States

        3. President Nominal by Art 74

          Governor Nominal by Art 163

        4. Except for his Discretionary Power he has to act on the A7A of CoM

        5. He has more Constitutional Discretionary Power than the President

        1. He has a Dual Role

        2. Executive Head of State

        3. Representative of Center

          SC directed to Not use the words Agent of Centre

        1. He is Appointed by the President
        1. His Term is of Five years (Occupies Office during Pleasure of the President)

        • Meaning

          Pleasure of President means on the Wish of President

        • B P Singhal Case, 2010

          • Questions he Asked ?

            He Challenged in Court of Law the Meaning of Pleasure of President

            Can the Governor be Removed at any point of time by the President ?

            Is there any remedy to the Governor ?

          • SC Said

            1. Yes the Governor Holds office only and only during the Pleasure of the President

            2. Meaning Governor can be removed at any point of time

              Although, the Pleasure of President is Mandatory, it does not mean the Governor can be removed Arbitrarily or by a Mala Fide Intention

              Such Removal is Subject to Judicial Review

            • Examples Arbitrary Actions

              1. President cant Remove if →

                Governor is NOT directing States for formulation of Policies according to Ideology of Centre

              2. If Gov is Removed if Centres Whims and Wishes aren’t followed

        • Security of Term of Governor

          1. Security of Term of President is needed for a Neutral & Better Functioning of President
          2. Governors Can only Handle the Pressure when there is a Security of their Term

        • Cases of Gubernatorial Tensions

          It is generally seen at places where the state govt is a local regional party or a party whose ideology doesn’t match with the centre

          For Example

          Kerala, West Bengal, Karnataka ETC

        • Commissions on Centre State Relations

          1. Sarkaria Commission, 1983

            1. It was established to Study the Centre State Relationship

            2. Recommended About Security of Tenure

            3. Governor to be Informed Reasons or Grounds of his removal IN WRITING

          2. Punchi Commission, 2007

            1. It was established to Study the Centre State Relationship
            2. Recommended a Fixed Tenure of 5 Years
            3. Removal of Governor by Impeachment
        1. Conditions of Office of Governor
          1. Should not be an MP of Member of State Legislature

          A Member can be appointed but as soon as he becomes Governor he shall cease to

          1. Should not hold any Office of Profit

          According to Jaya Bacchan Case

          1. Salaries and Allowances shall be determined by an Act of Parliament
          1. Same Person can be appointed Governor for Two or More States

          He will get a Salary of One State but he will get some extra allowances

          this will be decided by President

          1. He is Administered Oath by Chief Justice of High Court or the Next Senior Most Judge of State

    • Powers & Functions of Governor

        1. Governors doesn't exercise these powers
        2. No Diplomatic Powers
        3. No Military Powers
        4. No Emergency Powers
        1. Governors has Similar Powers to that of President
        Executive, Legislative, Ordinance & Pardoning Powers are synonymous with president with certain exception

      • Comparison between President & Governor wrt Pardoning Powers

        Executive Power of Centre Extends Means - The Law is Made by Parliament

        Executive Power of State Extends Means - The Law is Made by State Legislature

        | Theme | President | Governor | | --- | --- | --- | | Power by | Art 72 | Art 161 | | Idea Behind Pardoning Power | Same | Same | | Powers are | Pardon Commute Remit Reprieve Respite | Pardon Commute Remit Reprieve Respite | | Pardoning Power Jurisdiction | 1. Court Martial

        1. Executive Power of Union Extends → 2.1 Where Law is made by Parliament (Union List)

        2. In Case of Capital Punishment (Does Not Matter if Executive Power of Union Extends or Not, Capital Punishment is an exception for Pardoning to President)

        In Case of the Punishment Given on violation of above Laws, Pardoning Plea can be applied | 1. No Power wrt Court Martial

        1. Executive Power of State Extends →
        2.1 Where Law is Made by State Legislature (State List)

2.2 Where Law is made by Parliament && State Implements it (Concurrent List)

        2.3 Where Law is made by Parliament but centre asks the state to Implement it (Union List)

        1. In Case of Capital Punishment (Provided Executive Power of State Extends) OR (by Law Made by State Legislature)

        In Case of the Punishment Given on violation of above Laws, Pardoning Plea can be applied | | To Note | Aid & Advice of CoM | Aid & Advice of CoM | | To Note | Subject to Judicial Review | Subject to Judicial Review | | To Note | No Rights for Oral Hearing | No Rights for Oral Hearing |

        Special Case of Capital Punishment

        1. Capital Punishment is given by Law Made by State
          1. Can Apply for Pardoning Power to Governor
          2. Can Apply for Pardoning Power to President
          3. Both Independently
        2. Capital Punishment is given by Law Made by Parliament
          1. Can Apply for Pardoning Power to President Only

        Hence Governor has no Power in Pardoning in Cases of

        1. No Power wrt Court Martial
        2. No Power wrt cases where Executive Power of Union Extends

      • Comparison between President & Governor wrt Discretionary Power

        | Theme | President | Governor | | --- | --- | --- | | Constitutional Discretion | Less | More | | Situational Discretion | Both Exercise | Both Exercise | | Situational Discretion | Art 74 A&A of CoM | Art 163 A&A of CoM except in those areas where he can act in his discretion | | | There shall be a Council of Ministers with the Prime Minister at the head to aid and advise the President who shall, in the exercise of his functions, act in accordance with such advises | There shall be a CoM to A&A Gov to exercise his function except in so far as matter he is required to exercise his function in his discretion

        except in so far as matter he is required to exercise his function in his discretion - Explicit Recognition of his her constitutional Discretion |

      • Examples Where, Governor uses his Extra Discretionary Power

        • Art 200

          Reserving Bills for Consideration of President

        • Art 356

          Imposing President Rule i.e Sending report to Presidents

        • Art 167

          Can Summon Ministers and ask them

        • Art 200

          Reserving the Bill to be sent to the President

      • Due to this Discretion Power, There is a Issue as well

        State Govt Becomes Helpless

        There’s always a Lacuna on the question that Art 163 doesn’t define exact places where governor can exercise his discretion

        SC has said many time that governors discretion should only in the places where constitution has defined the Discretion clearly

        Despite this, even if the Gov doesnt act according to A&S of CoM then State Govt becomes helpless. It has no power to remove the Gov whatsoever, unlike the president who can be impeached by the parliament, at least there's a procedure

        Here the Governor is appointed and removed only by the President on A&A of CoM of Centre

        Way Forward

        1. Hence Recommendation of Sarkaria and Punchi Commission is a must to be Implemented
        2. Also, the Office of Governor should be Given a Specific Time Of Office
        3. CM of the State should be consulted before appointing the Gov

    • Difference Between Parliamentary and Presidential Parliament

      | Parliamentary System | Presidential System | | --- | --- | | Political Party with the Majority forms the Govt Eg : PM is Leader of Majority Party | President Directly / Indirectly elected by the People | | Executive Part of the Legislature | Executives are not Part of Legislature | | Executives Require Support of Legislature for Survival | Executives don't require any support | | Lower House can be dissolved before expiry of the term | House cant be Dissolved | | Less Stable | More Stable | | More Accountable | Less Accountable | | Dual Executive Head of State - President Head of Govt - PM | Real Executive Head of State & Govt - President | | No Complete Separation of Power Especially b/w Legislature & Executive | There is a Complete Separation of Power |

    • Difference Between Indian and British Parliament

      | Indian Parliamentary System | British Parliamentary System | | --- | --- | | Republic - Head of State Elected | Constitutional Monarchy - from Royal Family by Hereditary Succession | | Supremacy of Constitution | Parliamentary Sovereignty | | Any Person can be appointed as minister (Max 6 Months) Ex: Mamta & Uddhav | Only MP can be appointed as Minister | | PM can be from any House Ex: Manmohan | PM should be from only Lower House | | No Tradition of Shadow Cabinet | Tradition/Convention of Shadow Cabinet |

      PS : The Shadow Cabinet is the team of senior spokespeople chosen by the Leader of the Opposition to mirror the Cabinet in Government
