    • Attorney General & Advocate General

      • Appointment

        1. Choice is of Govt
        2. Should be Qualified to a Judge of SC
        3. Salary Decided by President

      • Functions needed to Perform

        1. He has to defend govt of India in Court of Law

          Example

          1. He Defended India in the Rafael Deal

          2. He Defended India in the Aadhaar Act

            It was introduced as a money bill

        2. He gives Legal Opinions wrt Variou Cases to the Government

        3. Any Other Functions as may be directed by the President

        4. in Art 143, Presidential Reference to SC by President, AG makes clear of the case to the SC

      • Immunities of Attorney General

        1. Can be called upon to Defend Govt in Parliament

          Opposition said Govt & Ministers don’t know, call AG, Govt didn’t accept

          He can talk and debate but he will not use the Rt to Vote

          Here he will Exercise all privileges and immunities as that of Member of Parliament except the right to vote

        2. He has the Right to be an Audience in any court within territory of India

        3. He Also has the Right to Private Practise but he should not hold against a Brief against the Govt

        4. He May also appear against the Govt, but with Prior Permission of Govt

        5. SC Maintained that the relation between AG and Govt is NOT that of a Employer and Employee but that of a Council and a Subject

      • Tenure

        Normally Term is 3 Years. They can be removed at any point of time on the pleasure of president

      • Points of Attorney General and Advocate General are same but with some minute changes of area

  • Legislature - Union & State Legislature

    • Schema of Legislature

    • Structure of the Parliament

      1. Parliament = President + Lok Sabha + Rajya Sabha
      2. President is not a member of parliament but he is a Integral Part of Parliament
      3. Bicameralism Nature of State
        1. Council of States - Upper Houses - Will of States
        2. House of People - Lower Houses - Will of People
    • Council of States (Rajya Sabha)

      • Why has it been made mandatory in the constitution ?

        1. To Protect the Interests of the States
        2. Strengthens the Federal Structure of Constitution

      • Special Powers of RS

        1. Art 312 - Creation of New All India Services - 3
        2. Art 249 - under some special circumstances the RS can authorise the LS to make laws on state list

      • Maximum Strength

        1. Elected → Not More than 238 from

          1. States →

            All States Have Representation in RS

          2. UT →

            Except for UT of PC, NCT of Delhi, J&K

          3. Details of Which are Present in the Schedule 4 of the Constitution

            Past and Future Amendments will be done on the premise of their populations

        2. Nominated → 12 from Areas of Literature, Art, Social Service, Science and Literature

          Examples : Sachin & PT Usha, Ellai Raja

      • Indirect Election of RS

        1. Electoral College - Elected Members of Legislative Assemblies

        2. Proportional Representation by means of Single Transferable Vote

          Proportional Representation

          1. Concept

            UP - 403 Seats

            BJP - 250

            SP - 100

            Others - 53

          2. Vacancy is RS happens after every second year, Hence Elections of 1/3 seats will be done every 2 Years

        3. The Maths of Election is

          1. Total No of Rajya Sabha Seats is decided in the Schedule 4 of the Constitution, for UP it is - 31
          2. Total No of Seats in the State Legislative Assembly of UP is 403
          3. Hence, (403/10+1)+1 → “38 Single First Preference Votes” are needed for a member to be elected to the Rajya Sabha
          4. BJP has 250 Seats in State Leg Ass, Hence BJP can send 250/38 → 6.54 → 6 Members by means of “38 Single First Preference Votes”
    • House of People (Lok Sabha)

      • Earlier

        Earlier It was 552 Members Maximum = 550 Elected + 2 Nominated

        Art 331 - Representation of Anglo Indian Community

        2 Members to be Nominated from Anglo Indian Community

        Now, 104th CAA Ended 2 Nominated Members

        Now It is of no use as, it has been repealed

      • Presently, Maximum Seats is 550 = 550 Elected (Sub to Art 331)

        1. 530 States - Elected Directly
        2. 20 UT - Elected Directly
        3. Current Total No of Seats - 543
        4. Hence India Needs to be divided into 543 Constituencies

      • Direct Election (UAF - Art 325 & Art 326 )

        1. By Art 326 - Adult Suffrage
          1. Earlier 21 Years
          2. By 61 CAA
          3. Now 18 Years
        2. By Art 325 - Universal
          1. Gives Equality Between the Adults

    • Territorial Constituency / Single Member Constituencies

      • FPPS - First Past the Post System

        There is no Threshold of votes as such

        He has to get Maximum No of Votes as compared to others, even if its just one

      • Seats are allocated in Proportion to the Population

        1. States with More Population will have more seats
        2. States with Less Population will have seats

    • Delimitation Commission

      • Who Undertakes this Creation of Constituency and allocation of Seats

        1. Concept

          Constitution Empowers the Parliament

          Parliament Makes the Law to Create a Authority

          this Authority has to take care of dividing India into number of constituencies

        2. Parliament Passed the Delimitation Act, 1952

        3. Delimitation Act Created Delimitation Commission

        4. Delimitation Commission has to create these constituencies based on the Representation by Population

        5. This Commission gets to know the Population by Means of Census of 1971 & 2001

      • Structure of Delimitation Commission
        1. Retired Judge of Supreme Court - Chairperson
        2. CEC / EC - Chief Election Commissioner
        3. SEC - State Election Commissioner
      • Functions of Delimitation Commission

        1. They have to decide the Number of Seats of Every State and UT by the Principle of RBP (Representation by Population

          • Here Census used is - For Inter State Parity - 1971 Census
            1. “Latest Census” was amended by 42nd CAA 1976
            2. Latest Census was freezed to 1971 Census
            3. This was amended by 84th CAA, 2001
            4. Now 1971 Census was fixed until first Census after 2021 (probably 2031)

        2. They have to Delimit the Constituencies = They have to draw the Boundaries of Constituencies

          For Ex : LS Seats for UP is 80 Seats

          • Here Census used is - For Intra State Parity - 2001 Census

            By Means of Delimitation Act of 2002

            2001 Census - used to Demarcate Boundary

        3. They have to Reserve Seats for SC & ST

          Here Seats will be reserved on the basis of Population

          • Here Census used is - For Intra Parity State - 2001 Census

            By Means of Delimitation Act of 2002

            2001 Census - used to allot seats to SC ST

    • Seats of RS, LS & SL are mentioned In

      1. Seats of RS - In Constitution
      2. Seats of LS & State Legislatures is in - In RPA, 1950

    • Tenure of Houses

      • Lok Sabha
        1. 5 Years Normally
        2. Reduced
          1. Lost Majority
          2. They have majority they ask the President to dissolve the Lok Sabha
        3. Term Extended -
          1. National Emergency under Art 352
          2. Third Emergency 1971 until 1977 due to Emergency
      • Rajya Sabha
        1. It’s a Permanent House
        2. Every Second Year 1/3 of the Members Retire
        3. Started in 1952
        4. First Batch Retired in 1954 - 1/3 Members
        5. Second Batch in 1956 - 2/3 Members Retired

    • Sessions of Parliament

    • What Happens to Bills

      • Summary

      • The Rule

        🧠 Rule : Any Bill Related to Lok Sabha, On Dissolution of Lok Sabha, It will Lapse

        With Exception of : If Before LS is Dissolved & President is Involved

      • Case 1 - Bill Lapse Subject to Few Exceptions
        1. in 17th LS, A Bill has underwent 3 Stages
        2. Bill Progressed → 2 Stages Pending
        3. 17th LS Dissolved, 18th LS Started
        4. Hence the Bill Lapses
      • Case 2
        1. RS may accept with change - RS will send back to LS - Bill Lapses
        2. RS accepts without any change - Sent to President - Bill Does Not Lapse

      • Bill Related to Lok Sabha It Will Lapse

        1. Yes
          1. It Will Lapse
            1. Exception - Before the Lok Sabha is Involved - Bill will NOT Lapse
        2. No
          1. It will not Lapse

      • Cases

        Pending = Under Consideration

        Bill Will Lapse

        • All Bills Pending in the Lok Sabha
          1. Intro in LS → Pending in LS → It will Lapse
          2. Intro in RS → RS Pass → Pending in LS → It will Lapse
        • All Bills pending in Rajya Sabha passed by Lok Sabha

          1. Intro is LS → Passed by LS → Pending in RS

            1. RS Passed it → Bill becomes an act
            2. RS Send it back for Reconsideration & Changes and Accepted to LS
            3. RS Rejected the Bill

            In all these 3 Cases the Bill Will Lapse

        Bill Will Not Lapse

        • Bill Pending in RS → Not Passed by LS

          Intro in RS → Pending in RS → Bill will NOT Lapse

        • Bills Pending for President Assent

          1. Bill Intro → Pass by RS → Pass by LS → Prez Generally used Absolute Veto Art 111 → Bill will NOT Lapse

        • Bill Sent for Re Consideration by the President

          1. Bill Intro → Pass by RS → Pass by LS → President Sent for Reconsideration → Bill will NOT Lapse

        • When LS and RS reach a Deadlock → On Art 74 → under Art 108 Prez can call a Joint Sitting → If Prez has called for Joint Sitting → Bill will Not Lapse

    • Qualification to the Members of Parliament for both of LS & RS

      • By Grounds Mentioned in Constitution

        1. They Should be a Citizen of India

        2. They have to take an Oath / Affirmation before an authorised authority means

          Authorised Authority → As Appointed by Election Commission Of India → Generally Called the Returning Officer

        3. Not Less than

          1. Lok Sabha - 25 Years of Age
          2. Rajya Sabha - 30 Years of Age

      • By Other Qualification as mentioned by Law

        Representation of People Act, 1950, Provides for Certain Qualifications like

        1. He Himself should be an elector from any constituency in India
        2. Members of Reserved Category to Contest Reserved Constituency (of SC/ST) → he himself should belong to a particular community

      • Difference in Qualification of LS & RS

        1. Age
        2. There is no Reserved Seats in Rajya Sabha

    • Disqualification to the Members of Parliament for both of LS & RS

      • Grounds Mentioned in Constitution

        1. If it is found that who is elected but is found that he was not a Citizen of India, leads to disqualification

        2. If he Voluntarily Gives up the Citizenship after becoming a Member of Parliament

        3. Undischarged Insolvent

          Somebody who has taken credit from Banks or from any financial institution and he is not paying the debt despite many notices to him by the Bank by Means of Insolvency Code

          He is considered to be a Disqualified Person

        4. Holds Office of Profit

          1. Parliament (Prevention) of Disqualification Act, 1959
          2. By Protection under this Law, Certain Offices are mentioned under this particular Law will not be considered as office of profit

        5. If somebody turns out to be a Unsound Mind

      • By Laws Made by Parliament

        1. By RPA, 1950 → there are no of grounds as of now, there’s only one ground

          If somebody is convicted under any offence, under any law for 2 or more years

          He will be considered Disqualified

        2. Found Guilty of Election Offences

          Example : Raj Narayan Case

        3. Here the Disqualification is Automatic

      • Disqualification By

        Disqualifying Authority is President of India on the Aid & Advice of Election Commissioner which is binding on the President

    • Anti Defection Law by 52nd CAA, 1985 (Tenth Schedule) - Special Case of Anti Defection Law

      • Introduction

        It was not a part of Original Constitution

        In Anti Defection Law, Disqualification is done by Presiding Officer himself, He Conducts an Independent Enquiry.

        He does not act on aid and advice of EC by a Quasi Judicial Process

        Here President has no say in the process

      • What is the Constitutional Position of Anti Defection Law ?

        Added by 52nd CAA, 1985 to the Tenth Schedule

      • Background of Anti Defection Law

        • Why was there no Defection Law in the Original Constitution

          In the original constitution there was no need for defection law

          1. Congress was only Party
          2. Conscience of people was high

        • What is the Meaning of Defection ?

          If a member having a ticket from one party to another party, after getting elected from the previous party

        • Why was a Rise in Defection Seen ?

          1. Coalition Govt
          2. Political Instability
          3. Aayaram Gayaram Episode

        • Why was Anti Defection Law Brought in

          1. To Prevent Political Instability
          2. To Prevent Political and Economic Gains of Defectors
          3. To Prevent Subversion of Entire Democratic Process
          4. To Respect the Sentiments and Emotions of Peoples Mandate

      • What is Anti Defection Law ?

        • What do you mean by Defection ?

          In general MP and MLA elected from a Particular Party ticket moving to another party OR engaging in anti party activities may be considered defection

        • What amounts to Defection ?

          • Voluntarily giving up membership of political party

            1. Resigned from the Party
            2. He has not resigned in a party but is engaging in activities against the party
            3. Example : BSP Member Not Resigned, Sitting in SP

          • If we abstain from voting or vote, contrary to party directions

            1. Party Whip - Senior Member of Party
            2. Anybody Doing Contrary to the Party Will

            Exception

            1. Taken Permission from Party Before Hand
            2. Apologised to the Party within 15 Days

          • If any independent member joins any political party

            1. Independent Member - निर्दलीय
            2. He can support the Govt or he can help in formation of Govt but without taking membership of the party

          • If any nominate member joins any political party after expiry of six months

            As of Now, RS 12 Members

            For Ex: At time of Nomination, Sachin Tendulkar was not a member of any party, ab within six months if he wants to join the party he can, but within 6 months

        • Example of Process

          1. Belonging to Congress
          2. Mp of 2 Years
          3. Goes to BJP
          4. Presiding Officer Disqualified Him
          5. He cannot be a member of Parliament
          6. Unless and Until he is Re Elected Again

      • Exception to Anti Defection Law

        1. If there is 1/3rd Split in the Membership of the Party

          Case 1

          Party - 60 Members

          1/3 - Move Out - 20

          2/3 Remain - 40 Members

          This is called as a Faction or Ideological Differences

          Case 2

          This was amended by 91st CAA

          Brought in 2/3rd Merger

          A Minimum of 40 Members have to split and get merged with another party

      • What are the Case Laws Related to ADL ?

        Kihoto Hollohan Case, 1992

        • Argument 1
          1. It Challenged the Constitutionality of ADL
          2. It argued that ADL violates Art 105 in Centre and Art 194 and takes away parliamentary privileges of the MP
          3. It violates Freedom of Speech in voting in Parliament to which they are immune to
          4. SC Said, that there is grave conflict but Stability of govt is more important over the Freedom of Speech
          5. Hence, its constitutionality was upheld
        • Argument 2
          1. 52nd CAA, 1985 said that decision of presiding officer to be final and binding
          2. SC maintained that the law in entirety was constitutional but the part of presiding officers decision being final and binding is unconstitutional
          3. Hence this was changed by SC
          4. now a member can appeal to higher courts

      • What are the Pros and Cons of ADL ?

        Advantages of Anti-Defection Law are

        1. Ensures stability of government
        2. Ensures Party Discipline and Decisions on Party Direction

        Disadvantages of Anti-Defection law

        1. One of the Reason that why quality of debate in parliament has reduced is because the mentality of MP has become

          No Matter what he debates, he has to anyhow vote on the party lines

        2. Not Truly Representative : MP Depicts the Will of Party and Not will of People

        3. Mis Use of Discretionary Powers by the Presiding Officer

          Speaker i.e Presiding Officer is from the Ruling Party

          There is not a Fixed timeline for the Presiding Officer to Act, SC Recommended the Presiding Officer to act within 3 Months

          The Speaker can not take Suo Moto Cognisance

          • Example TRS & TDP In Telangana

      • What are the Suggestions to improve upon ADL ?

        1. Strictly Restrict ADL to No Confidence Motion

          Example : UK, Parliament System

          Law Commission of Indian in its 170th Report also recommended this

        2. Power to be Exercise by independent officers like President and Governors on the Recommendation of ECI

          Suggestion by Dinesh Goswami Committee & M Venkatachaliah Commission (NCRWC, 2002)

          NCRWC - National Commission to Review Working of Constitution

        3. Appoint a Politically Neutral Person as Speaker

          Ex : Convention is followed in UK

          Once a Person is elected he gives up the membership of Party

        4. SC Recommended Creation of Separate Tribunal to deal cases with ADL

    • Speaker of Lok Sabha

      • Presiding Officers of

        Lok Sabha - Speaker

        Rajya Sabha - Vice President

      • The Process of Election of Speaker

        Election

        Prove Majority

        PM Appointed by President

        CoM Appointed by Prez on A&A of PM

        Prez Appoints Speaker Pro Term

        Speaker Pro Term Administers the Oath to Newly Elected Members

        Speaker Pro Term will conduct elections for a Speaker on the Date Decided by President

      • Qualification

        Only Qualification to Contest Elections is that he or she should be a member of Lok Sabha

        To be elected as Speaker, a simple majority is needed

        Office of Speaker Pro Term ceases to exist after Speaker is elected

      • Pro Term Speaker

        Speaker Pro Term is generally a person who has been elected to the House Maximum No of times generally

      • Tenure

        The Current Speaker of 17th LS will continue to be the Speaker until 18th LS, until Pro Term Speaker of 18th LS

      • Removal

        He can be removed as speaker by means of effective majority

      • Resignation

        Speaker can give his resignation to Deputy Speaker

      • Disqualification

        All Criteria of MP for Disqualification

        • Exception

          A Speaker cannot be disqualified from the post of speaker for resigning from the Party

      • Powers & Function of the Speaker

        1. Maintain the Order and Decorum in the House

        2. Presiding Officer has the power to carry out the proceedings only if there is Quorum of the House

          Quorum - Minimum Strength Needed to carry the Proceedings of House

          Quorum of Lok Sabha -1/10 of the Total Membership

        3. He is the Final Authority to Interpret rules within the House

        4. He has the Power to Preside over Joint Sitting

          President has the Power to call for Joint Sitting under Art 108 on A&A of CoM

          In Joint Sitting if it is passed with Simple Majority, then it is deemed to be passed by both houses of Parliament

          Convened by President, Presided by Speaker

          If Speaker not There, Deputy Speaker of LS, Dy Chairman of RS

          Chairman RS cannot

        5. He has the Power to Certify a Bill as a Money Bill under Art 110

        6. He has the Powers to Disqualify on the grounds of Defection

        7. Voting Power of Speakers

          To Break a Tie he can use or exercise his casting vote

        8. He Appoints Chairman to Various Committees.

        9. He himself is the Chairman of

          1. Rules Committee
          2. Business Advisory
          3. General Purpose Committee

      • Independence and Impartiality of the Speaker

        • How his position can be misused

          1. Important because of Anti Defection Power

          2. Misuse in case of certifying a bill as a Money Bill

            In Money Bill RS deprived of its power and LS will have all the Power

          3. Misuse of Power to not give opposition member be spoken

        • Provisions for Speaker

          1. Security of Tenure

          2. Salary & Allowance charged from CFI (Consolidated of Fund of India)

            CFI - Consolidated Found of India

            Charged on CFI - Parliament can’t

            Made from CFI

          3. Not to be Questioned in court of Law Regarding proceedings of house

          4. Presiding Officer can vote in case of a Tie as his Casting Vote

    • Deputy Speaker of Lok Sabha
      1. Its a Parliamentary Convention from the 11th LS &
      2. From 16th & 17th LS there is No Deputy Speaker as there is No Opposition as of Now
      3. Elected from Amongst the Members
      4. Acts as a Speaker when Office of Speaker is Vacant
      5. He automatically becomes chairperson of a committee if he is a member of that committee given that speaker is not a member of that party
    • Chairman of Rajya Sabha
      • Vice President - Ex Officio Chairman
      • Powers & Functions (Similar to Speaker)
        1. Maintain Order and Decorum
        2. Conduct Proceedings when Quorum
        3. Final Authority to Interpret Rules of RS
        4. Final Authority to Preside Joint Sittings
        5. No Power to Certify Money Bills
        6. No Power to Preside Joint Sitting
    • Deputy Chairman of Rajya Sabha
      1. Elected Amongst Members of Rajya Sabha
      2. Removal by Effective Majority
      3. Resignation to Chairman

    • Parliamentary Privileges (Art 105 & Art 194)

      • What does it mean by Parliamentary Privileges ?

        Parliamentary privilege refers to rights and immunities enjoyed by parliamentarians in order to properly discharge the duties and functions entrusted upon them by the Constitution

        Roles of Parliamentarians

        To Make Laws

        Accountability of the Govt

        Financial Accountability

      • What is the Rationality behind it ?

        To Ensure Accountability of Government

      • Constitutional Provisions ?

        For Centre Art 104 and State Art 194

      • What are the Parliamentary Privileges exercised by MP’s ?

        2 by Constitutional Provision and others by Parliamentary Convention

        1. Right to Freedom of Speech and Right to Vote

        2. Right to Publication - Collective Right

          Collective Privilege is exercised by means of speaker

          What Punishment will be given - Parliamentary will decide

        3. But Scope of Parliament is not Restricted to these two privileges,

          Apart from these Priv there may be other Priv as may be determined by Laws of Parliament

        4. Till the Time Parliament Makes Laws, Parliamentarians may exercise privileges before constitution came into existence(i.e by Constituent Assembly)

      • Important Note on Parliamentary Privileges

        1. Constitution Empowers the Parliament to Codify Privileges
        2. Till Such Codification Members Continue to Enjoy Privileges they enjoyed before the commencement of constitution
        3. It is taken care by Privilege Committee, Each Separate for Lok Sabha and Rajya Sabha

      • Final List of Privileges exercised by Parliamentary

      • What are the Issues Around Parliamentary Privileges ?

        Borrowed from British Parliamentary System as a Conventional Practise

        • Collective
          1. Art 105 - Ind & Coll
          2. Freedom to Right of Publication of Debates Proceedings or to Restrain Them
          3. To Punish Members and Outsiders for Breach of its Privileges
          4. Right to Exclude Strangers and Outsiders
        • Individual
          1. Art 105
          2. Freedom of Speech - Individual → Subject to Exception of
            1. Rules of House of Parliament & Constitution
            2. Unparliamentary Languages
            3. Cannot Engage in Contempt of Court or Criticise Actions of a Judge
          3. Exemption from arrest 40 Days before and after the session → Effectively Amounts to 5 Years of No Arrest

      • What are the Issues Around Parliamentary Privileges & Associated Case Laws ?

        Violation of Art 19 & Art 21 of Constitution

        • MSM Sharma Case, 1959 or Searchlight Newspaper Case

          Case of Bihar, Published in Newspaper which was barred by Speaker of Leg of Bihar

          Here Supreme Court Ruled that Art 194 will have precedence over Art 19

        • Keshav Singh Case, 1964

          Ques: Is Judicial Review of Parliamentary Privileges Possible ?

          Ans: SC Maintained that Judicial Review is Possible in case of Parliamentary privileges especially if it deprives Rt to Life & Liberty

          Court Said that whenever you are trying to deprive someone of Rt to Life and Liberty, there has to be Due Process of Law

          Ques: Can Judges be considered to have Breach of Parliamentary Privileges

          Ans: No they cannot be ever a Liability to the Judges

      • Why is Codification Required ?

        1. Goes Against the Idea of Limitation of Powers

        2. Goes Against the Principle of Constitutional Supremacy

          It rather promotes Parliamentary Sovereignty

        3. For Protection of Fundamental Rights

        4. Misused for Vested Interests - Generally for Political Vendetta

      • What are the Disadvantages of Codifying Parliamentary Privileges ?

        Once Codified, it will lead to Judicial Scrutiny and Lead to Pendency of Cases and May be used to hamper the proceedings of parliament

        M N Venkatachaliah Commission (NCRWC 2002) - Recommended the Codification of Parliamentary Privileges

      • Sample Mains Question

    • Parliamentary Procedures - Summarise, Structure and Add Once

      In Sequence

      • Summary

      • Question Hour

        • Introduction

          1. Any Kind of Question can be fired to Ministers
          2. It is also called Accountability Hour
          3. 15 Days Notice has to be given to the presiding officer

        • Types of Questions asked

          Advance Notice has to be given to the Minister

          1. Starred Question - Oral Answer

            Here Notice time is a Minimum of 15 Days Notice

          2. Unstarred Questions - Written Answer

            Here Notice time is a Minimum of 15 Days Notice

          3. Short Notice Question - Oral Answer

            Here Notice time can be less than 10 Days

      • Zero Hour

        1. Merely an Extension of Question Hour
        2. No Prior Notice of 10 Days is Needed
        3. Indian Innovation, Its Merely a Convention
        4. Starts at Zero at 12 Noon
        5. Entire Rules and Procedures have to be done by speaker Lok Sabha
        6. There is No Specific Agenda for the Zero Hour as Such

      • Motions & Questions

        • Difference between Motion and Questions in the House

          1. Question is Asked and it is Answered

            It is introduced in the first part of the day

          2. Motion is Introduced, Debated and Deliberated & Voted Upon

            It is introduced in the second part of the day

        • Introduction to Motion

          A motion is a proposal which is introduced in the house to elicit a debate on a particular matter

          It can be put up only in the second part of the day

      • Types of Motions

        • Vote of Thanks

          • What is Motion of Thanks

            Given by President

          • When Does it Happen ?

            It is given by By President

            1. When Govt is Installed after General Election is Over

              He Talks about Objectives and Aims of the Government for the next 5 Years

            2. First Session of Every Year

              He Lays Report like a Progress Report of the Government

          • What will happen if the Motion is Not Passed ?

            1. In Lok Sabha - Considered as No Confidence Motion -
            2. In Rajya Sabha - Its Just an Embarrassment if government takes it to be an Embarrassment
        • No Confidence Motion
          1. This word is no where mentioned in the Question
          2. Mentioned in Rule 198 of Lok Sabha
            1. Any Person can Introduce No Confidence Motion
            2. But it can be in the Lok Sabha Only
            3. Presiding Officer may or may not accept it
            4. PO may not accept it unless and until it is supported by 50 Members at the time of introduction
            5. There has to be No Specific Agenda for the No Confidence Motion
            6. Once No Confidence Motion Passes
              1. President may accept the resignation and look for installation of new government
              2. President may accept the resignation but if there is no new govt to be installed the old govt may act as a Caretaker Government. Here A&A of CoM will not work
          3. Art 75 of the Constitution - Talks about collective responsibility of members or minsters to Lok Sabha
        • Adjournment Motion
          1. Not Mentioned in Constitution
          2. It is a Rule of the House
          3. Can be Introduced only in the Lok Sabha
          4. Once this motion is admitted in the floor of the house, even the speaker lok sabha cant adjourn the house, until and unless the ADJOURNMENT Motion is talked and debated and completed
          5. It is used for a Matter of Urgent Public Importance
          6. Can only be introduced only if 50 Members support this motion then only it can be introduced
          7. Minimum Discussion will be for 2.5 Hours
          8. House cannot be adjourned Motion is put to vote
          9. Even if it is passed, there is no impact on the Government but it is a indication that government might not be having majority
        • Privilege Motion
          1. Before Punishing a Individual, this motion is introduced
          2. Motion is passed and voted upon to finalise the punishment for breach of Parliamentary Privileges

      • Lunch Break

      • Actual Business of House

    • The Law Making Process - As a Base by Example of Ordinary Bill

      What is a Bill

      It’s a Legislative Proposal, Once Passed by Both the Houses of Parliament and has Been Given Presidential Assent before it become a Law

      Procedure Involved in the Bill

        1. Introduction of the Bill

        Who Introduces the Bill

        • Government Bill - Introduced only by the Minster
          • Pre Legislative Process - Add from Lecture
            1. Bill is referred to Legislative Dept under Law Ministry for the drafting of Bill
            2. If he wants, Before he places in House, it can be put up in public domain
            3. Before putting it in parliament, PM is told about it
            4. PM calls a Cabinet Meeting with Important Ministers
            5. Cabinet Gives Approval - Bill is backed by the Government
            6. Now Treasury has to support the Bill, if he denies it will be considered as Defection
        • Private Member Bill
          • Pre Legislative Process - Add from Lecture
            1. Anyone who is not a minster is a Private Member
            2. He has to do it by Legislative Experts Privately
            3. He will have to take permission of Presiding Officers and put it up in the house

        What is the Subject Matter of the Bill

        1. Money Bill
        2. Financial Bill
        3. Ordinary Bill
        4. Constitutional Bill

        What is the Place where Bill has been Introduced

        1. Lok Sabha
        2. Rajya Sabha

        What happens with the Bill

        1. The Person who Introduces this Bill will discuss the Objectives of the Bill

        1. Referral to DRSC

        2. Departmentally Related Standing Committees

        3. In Parliament - 24 DRSC Present

          Each House will have 31 Members 21 from - Lok Sabha

          10 from - Rajya Sabha

          1. 16 DRSC - Lok Sabha
          2. 8 DRSC - Rajya Sabha

        4. These DRSC’s divide the Whole Major Functions of Government

        5. Example

          1. Constitution Amendment Bill - to DRSC on Home Affairs
          2. Mediation Bill - to DRSC on Public Grievances

        6. They Scrutinise in Depth and Prepare a Report on the Bill. They Give the Report back to the House

        7. Drawback of these Committee

          Neither it is mandatory for the Govt to refer to DRSC

          Nor the Recommendation of DRSC is Binding on the Govt

        8. A Minister cannot be a part of any DRSC

        1. Discussion Stage or Second Reading

        2. Clause by Clause Analysis is done here s

        3. Even after the Second Reading Stage Govt feels that bill should be referred to another committees

        4. It can be referred to - Ad Hoc Committee

          Ad Hoc Committee - Temporary Committee made for a Specific Time is called

          Standing Committee - Permanent Committee

          1. Join Parliamentary Committee (JPC)
          2. Select Committee - is from a Specific House not from Both the House

        5. Example : Surrogacy Bill became Act → It was long time pending in the Select Committee

        1. Final Vote or Third Reading in the First House
        2. Bill has to be totally accepted or rejected
        3. Bill has to be passed with Simple Majority
        4. No Clause by Clause Analysis
        5. No Changes can be made here in this Stage
        1. Bill in the Second House

        • The Process

          1. Ordinary Bill - Both LS and RS have Equal Power

          2. Bill is introduced in the Second House

            Major Objectives and Reasons of the Bill will be discussed

            1. They Do Not Take An Action
              1. If after the Introduction the Bill has been pending in the second house for more 6 Months → Constitution calls it Deadlock
            2. They Take an Action → Bill moves to Second Reading Stage

          3. Second Reading Stage in Second House - They Make Clause by Clause Analysis

          4. They can make changes

            They can refer it to a JPC or Select Committee

          5. They Do Not Make Changes

            Bill is sent to Third Reading Stage

          6. Bill is put to vote in the Second House

            1. Bill is Passed
              1. They Pass with Changes
                1. First House will Accept the Changes → Bill is sent to Presidents Assents
                2. First House will Not Accept the Changes → Deadlock
              2. They Pass with No Changes
                1. Bill Goes to the President for his Assent
            2. Bill is not Passed → Deadlock
            3. They do not take action since introduction for 6 Months → Acc to Constitution → Deadlock

        • The Summary

          Same Procedure as in the first house

          Options in the Hands of Rajya Sabha

          1. Accept the Bill without any change → Sent for President Assent
          2. Reject the Bill → Deadlock
          3. Pass with Amendments & First House Accepts the Amendment → Sent for Presidents Assent
          4. Pass with Amendments & First House Does Not Accepts the Amendment → Deadlock
          5. No Action for Six Months → Deadlock

          Here Joint Sitting can be convened by President under Art 108

          Presided by → Speaker LS → Dy Speak LS → Deputy Chairman RS

        1. Presidential Assent
        2. Can Give Assent to Bill → Bill becomes an Act
        3. Can withhold Assent to Bill → Absolute Veto
        4. Can send for Reconsideration to the House → Suspensive Veto
        5. Can sit on it without communicating on the Bill → Pocket Veto

    • Types of Bills & Allied Procedure (Detailed)

      • Difference Between Ordinary Bill, Money Bill, Financial Bills (1 & 2), Constitutional Bills

        | Theme of Difference | Ordinary Bill | Money Bill | Financial Bill 1 | Financial Bill 2 | Constitutional Amendment Bill | | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | | Powers of LS & RS | Lok Sabha & Rajya Sabha have equal powers | Lok Sabha is more powerful | Like Ordinary Bill | Like Ordinary Bill | | | Prior President Recommendation | No Prior Recommendation of President is Needed | Prior Recommendation of President is Needed | Prior Recommendation of President is Needed | President may recommend for its consideration in both the houses | Not Needed | | Introduced in | Can be Introduced in LS & RS | Can only be Introduced in LS | Can only be Introduced in LS | Like Ordinary Bill | Can be Introduced in LS & RS | | Can Second House Reject | RS can reject the bill or sit on it for 6 months until the deadlock | RS cannot reject the bill Can Sit on it for 14 Days | Like Ordinary Bill | Like Ordinary Bill | | | Recommendation Bill of RS Binding | The recommendation of RS has to be considered or else deadlock | The recommendation of RS is not binding & if LS does not agree, Money Bill can still pass | Like Ordinary Bill | Like Ordinary Bill | | | Presidents Assent | President Can

        1. Approve - Art 74 on A&A of CoM
        2. Withhold - Absolute Veto
        3. Sit-Pocket Veto
        4. Reconsideration - Suspensive | President cannot send it back for Reconsideration Only can Withhold the Assent → in case of Caretaker Govt in case of LS Dissolved | Like Ordinary Bill | President Can

        7. Sit-Pocket Veto
        8. Reconsideration - Suspensive | | | Scope of Deadlock | Possible

        10. RS Sends for Recommendations but LS Rejects
        11. RS Sits over it for 6 Months | No Scope for Deadlock | Like Ordinary Bill | Like Ordinary Bill | |

      • Ordinary Bills

        What is a Subject Matter of Ordinary Bill

        Any Bill which is not a Constitutional Amendment Bill, Nor a Money Bill, Nor a Finance Bill1, Nor a Finance Bill 2 will be considered as a Ordinary Bill

        Examples

        1. POCSO Act
        2. CAA

      • Money Bills - Art 110

        • Introduction

          1. Art 110 deals with Money Bills
          2. Any Bill which contains only those items mentioned in Art 110 of the Constitution can be certified as money bill by the Speaker of Lok Sabha

        • Subject Items in Art 110

          • Important Full Form

            1. CFI → Consolidated Fund of India

              Income Generated by Govt is Credited into CFI

              To Withdraw Money, Approval of Parliament is Needed

            2. CFI → Contingency Fund of India

            3. PAI → Public Accounts of India

          The 6 Majors Heading of Art 110 for Money Bill

            1. Taxation Matters

            No Tax can be Levied except by Authority of Law

            Ex : IT Act → Authorises Govt to collect Taxes

            1. Borrowing Clauses

            Indian Budget → Deficit Budget → Earning - Expenses = Deficit

            Law Needed → FRBM Act - Deals with Clauses of Borrowing

            1. Custody of Consolidated Fund and Contingency Fund of India AND Withdrawal of Money

            Income Generated by Govt is Credited into CFI

            To Withdraw Money, Approval of Parliament is Needed

            Contingency Fund of India - Expenditure for Unforeseen Expenditure like a Disaster

            It is Generally Less the Consolidated Fund

            1. Appropriation of Money from CFI

            Withdrawal from Contingency Fund of India can be authorised by merely a Executive Action under Contingency Fund of India Act, 1951

            1. Declaring a Expenditure as Charged Expenditure for CFI

            There can be only two types of Expenditure in Indian Government

            Under Art 112 of the Constitution - Tells which Expenditure are Charged Expenditure and Which are Made Expenditure

            1. Made Expenditure
            2. Charge Expenditure

            • Explanation

            1. Receipt of money on account of CFI or PAI

            PAI - Public Accounts of India

            Govt Functions as a Trustee to this Fund

            PAI → Example : Post Office Savings, Pension Funds

        • Procedure for a Money Bill

          • Detailed

            1. Introduction of Bill - Requires Prior Recommendation of President

              Both Minister and Private Members can introduce the Bill → But in both the case Prior Recommendation of President is Needed

              Prior Recommendation - To Discourage Private Members to Introduce Money Bill

            2. Can be Introduced only in the Lok Sabha → as Govt is Accountable only to the Lok Sabha

            3. Speaker Certifies the Bill to be a Money Bill → Now RS has no Power on this Bill

            4. Only Power of RS is → Max RS can sit over the Bill for 14 Days

              1. Can they Reject the Bill - No
              2. Can they accept the Bill
                1. With Changes → Send back to Lok Sabha → Can Send Back with Changes but Recommendation of Rajya Sabha is NOT binding upon Lok Sabha
                2. Without Changes
          • Summarised
            1. Introduced Only in the House of People
            2. Transmitted to Council of State after passed by Lok Sabah
            3. In RS Maximum Delay of 14 Days Allowed
            4. Return the Bill with or Without Recommendation
            5. Lok Sabha can accept or reject such recommendation

      • Relation Between Money and Financial Bill

      • Financial Bills

        • Relation Between Money Bills and Financial

          All Money Bills are Financial Bills

          But All Financial Bills are NOT Money Bills

          Now, other than the Money Bills there can be bills which deal with the Finances of Government. These can be divided into Two Types, Namely :

        • Financial Bills Type 1

          1. A Bill which contains any of the matters contained in Article 110 but does not Exclusively Contain only those Matters

            Meaning It will contain some clause of the 6 Subjects of Art 110 but not only that, with Art 110 Clauses it will also have some other Parts as well

          2. Example: Compensatory Afforestation Fund Act, 2016 → To Maintain Balance between Afforestation and Development in India

            For a Industrialist who wants to build a Industry in a Forest Area, he will pay a compensation for the Same. This Fund will be used for developing a forest cover equal or better than that

          3. This Fund will go to

            PAI - Public Authority Fund of India

            PAS - Public Authority Fund of State

          4. This Fund will be Managed by CAMPA like Officials Fees and Usage of this Money

          1. How is it Similar to Money Bill

            1. It can be Introduced only with the Prior Recommendation of President
            2. It can be Introduced only in the Lok Sabha

          2. President Assent

            Like Ordinary Bills

        • Financial Bills Type 2

          1. Any Bill which merely involves expenditure but does not Include any matter in Art 110
          2. Example: Salary Allowances and Pensions of Members of Parliament Bill
          1. Procedure for Financial Bill Type 2 is Similar to that of an Ordinary Bill except that President may Recommend for its consideration in Both the Houses
          2. That is After the Introduction in the House and in Between the Second Reading of the House
          3. In Ordinary Bill DRSC Can be Skipped and from Introduction it can directly go to Second Reading
          4. But in Financial Bill Type 2, After the Introduction Either After DRSC Step or to directly go to Second Reading Stage, Presidents Assent is Needed, It is a Must

      • Constitutional Bills

        Power & Procedure of Parliament to amend the Constitution

        Some Important Questions

        • Who can Introduce a Bill ?

          • Either a Minister
          • Private Member

        • Do they have to take Prior Permission from President ?

          No Not under Art 368

        • Do they have to take Permission from Chairman of the House ?

          Yes, a Mere Formality

          in LS : Speaker

          in RS : VP

        • Can it be Introduced in any house ?

          Yes, in either LS or RS

        Types of Constitutional Amendments

        • Bill tries to Amend - Non Federal Provisions - Doesn’t Change Power Balance b/w Centre & State
          • Procedure
            1. Bill has to be Introduced (by any MP) - Subject Matter is to Amend the Constitution
            2. Introduced in & Passes to → LS/RS || RS/LS
            3. It should gain Special Majority
              1. </ 2/3rd Members Present & Voting
              2. Absolute Majority of House Needed
            4. President has to Give the Assent ( No Veto ) - It was made mandatory for the President to give assent to the bill by 24th CAA, 1971
        • Bill tries to Amend - Federal Provisions - Changes Power Balance b/w Centre & State
          • Procedure
            1. Bill has to be Introduced (by any MP) - Subject Matter is to Amend the Constitution
            2. Introduced in & Passes to → LS/RS || RS/LS
            3. It should gain Special Majority in both the Houses Separately
              1. </ 2/3rd Members Present & Voting
              2. Absolute Majority of House Needed
            4. Then it is Sent for Ratification by the State Legislature
              1. <1/2 of State Legislatures Accept it, its Enough
              2. i.e 28 States - 14 State Legislatures have to Ratify them
                1. State Legislature - Simple Majority
                2. State Legislative - Simple Majority
            5. President has to Give the Assent ( No Veto ) - It was made mandatory for the President to give assent to the bill by 24th CAA, 1971
          • Example
            • Art 246
            • Schedule 7

    • Budget Bill - Annual Financial Statement

      • What is a Financial Year ?

        1 April to 31 March

      • What is a Budget

        Budget is Statement of Estimated Expenditure & Estimated Earnings

      • Some Important Points

        • When is the Budget Laid Down by the FM ?

          On Or After 2017 - Budget is Laid by Finance Minister on 1st Feb

        • Whose Responsibility is to Lay down the Budget ?

          Under Constitution - The Responsibility to Lay Down the Budget is of President of India

        • In Which Session is Put to Vote in the Parliament

          It is put to vote in the parliament in a Budget Session

        • Who Prepares the Budget and What is the Hierarchy ?

          1. Under Finance Ministry
          2. Under Secretary Department of Economic Affairs there is a HOD of Budget Division
          3. This is then Presented to the Finance Minister
          4. FM puts it to the PM who then approves it to be put to Parliament

        • When would the process for creating the Budget Start ?

          The Process for Budget of Year 2023 would be started by September 2022

        • Is the Budget Laid down in front of only LS or Both LS & RS ?

          Now President has to lay down such report in front of Both the Houses of Parliament every financial year

      • What are the Contents of a Budget

        Budget is a Statement in the Form of Estimated Receipt and Estimated Expenditure

        Expenditures have to be divided into Charged and Made ExpenditureThis has been told about in Art 112

        1. Made Expenditure - Around 95% Expenditures - Voted in LS
        2. Charged Expenditure - Around 5% of Total - Cant be Voted in LS

          1. Salary of President & His Office Expenditure

          2. Salary Allowances of Chairman, Dy Chairman, Speaker & Dy Speaker

          3. Debt Charges of the Government

          4. Salary Allowance and Pensions of Judges of Supreme Court

          5. Pension of Judges of High Court

            In Case of HC it is just the pensions

            What About Salary of Judges of HC → will be Consolidated Funds of Respective State

          6. Salary Allowance and Pension of CAG

          7. Sums Required to Satisfy any judgement, decree or any award by the court

            Example: SC Fined Govt. of Rs 50 Lac in Favour of Shri Nambi Narayanan

          8. Any Other Expenditure declared by a Law of Parliament or Constitution

            Its an Indicative List, Not an Exhaustive List

            Example Constitution: Art 146 → Servants Working in Supreme Court

            Or By a Law Made by Parliament

      • Process of Budget Enactment

        • Introduction of Budget

          In Both the Houses - First in LS and then After a Day or Two in RS

          FM Gives a Introductory Speech - Stating Some Basic Trends, Facts and Allocations

        • General Discussion Stage

        • Scrutiny by DRSC

          DRSC were started in 1990’s

          Total 24 DRSC - 16 LS & 8 RS → Major Functions of Govt are Covered Here

          1 DRSC - 31 Members = 21 Mem of LS + 10 Mem of RS

          Each DRSC will look into the Specific Part of the Budget

          For Example : Defence DRSC will look into the Defence Part of Budget Only

          This Generally Happens for 2 to 3 Weeks

          DRSC Submits the Report back to Lok Sabha

        • Vote on Demand for Grants

          1. These are Demands of the various Department one at a time and NOT the WHOLE Budget at a time
          2. It is only the Power of LS and NOT RS
          3. LS Passes all these Demands One by One By Simple Majority

            1. The LS can do the 4 things to a Demand

            2. They Can Approve the Motion

            3. Policy Cut Motion

              It Disapprove the Entire Demand

              The Demand be Reduced to Rs 1

            4. Economy Cut Motion

              Demand is Reduced by a Specific Amount as decided by Lok Sabha

            5. Token Cut Motion

              Last Year they had grievance with the way money was spent

              It is like with a warning, these motions is passed

              As a Symbol - Rs 1 is deducted from the Proposed Budget

            1. Lok Sabha has 26 Days to Pass All These Demands

            2. First 25 Days - Important Demands are Put to Vote One by One

            3. Last Day - All Undiscussed Demands are Put to Vote and Passed All together at once → It is called Guillotine Closure

            4. Here Only the Made Expenditure are Voted Upon. ONLY MADE EXPENDITURE CAN BE PUT TO VOTE

              Charged Expenditure Cannot be Voted Upon Even in Lok Sabha. They can be discussed but cannot be put to vote

          1. Why is the Prerogative of Lok Sabha

          Because Expense is the Responsibility

          1. Here In Principal Approval has been Given but the Permission to take out money hasn’t been given yet

        • Passing of Appropriation Bill (Money Bill)

          All the Procedures Seen in the Case of a Money Bill will be applied

          The Withdrawal and Deposit Authority of Govt is Finance Ministry

        • Passing a Finance Bill (Money Bill)

          1. How Much Tax Should be Credited in the CFI
          2. Deposit into CFI

      • Time Line of Budget

        • Prior 2017

          Railway Budget - First Week of February

          General Budget - Last Week of February / Last Day of February 28 Feb

        • Post 2017

          1. Introduction of Budget - 1st Feb
          2. General Discussion - 3 to 4 Days
          3. DRSC - 2 Weeks
          4. Vote on Demand - 26 Days

          45 Days Completed by Now

          Budget has to be Enacted before 1 April

        • Case Where Budget Cannot be Passed Before 1 April

          In the Whole Process It Approximately Takes 45 Days and at Max RS can sit on it for 14 Days

          Thus before 2017 if the budget is introduced on 28 Feb then, the complete enactment of Budget i.e the Passing of Appropriation Bill would not be possible before 1 April

        • Vote on Account

          It is a special power given to parliament to authorise the government to take out money from CFI even in case of Pending Passage of Appropriation Bill and Passing of Budget for Maximum 2 Months

          Only if at least the General Discussion Stage is completed

          and in the meantime Parliament has to enact the Budget

    • Types of Grants

      After Budget, If New Expenditure Occurs or Expenditure Increases we have certain tools in Indian Constitution

      • Supplementary Grant

        • Defintion

          It is the money granted found to be insufficient for a particular service during a financial year

        • Example: MGNREGA

          It happened in 2021,22,23 → because of Covid & Migration of Workers

          Budget was allocated to MGNREGA before covid. By December the Funds for the Scheme was over for that financial year.

          Now the Money was found to be Insufficient for Entire Course of Year

          Money can be made available to the program in the form of Supplementary Grant.

        • Process

          Hence, Govt will put a Vote on Demand for Grant, it will be put to vote and then Lok Sabha will approve it.

          This will be Followed by a Corresponding Appropriation Bill

          Note: The Scheme should already be there in the Budget

      • Additional Grant

        • Defintion

          Additional Expenditure on a New Service not contemplated in annual financial expenditure of Year

        • Example : COVID

          Example: Covid was unseen for

        • Process

          Hence, Govt will put a Vote on Demand for Grant, it will be put to vote and then Lok Sabha will approve it.

          This will be Followed by a Corresponding Appropriation Bill

      • Excess Grant

        • Defintion

          Money Spent on any service during a financial year in excess of the amount Granted

        • Example

          Defence 50,000 Cr

          Parliament Approved this way

          1. Salary 10,000 Cr
          2. Research & Acquisition 10,000 Cr
          3. Border Road Development 10,000 Cr
          4. Arms Acquisition 10,000 Cr
          5. Other Miscellaneous Charges 10,000 Cr

        • Process

          1. Transfer of the Money from One Head to Another Head
          2. First they Spend the Money
          3. They Then take the Approval of the Lok Sabha

    • Vote on Account, Vote of Credit, Exceptional Grant

      • Vote on Account

        Provision to make advance gran in respect of estimated expenditure before passing of the appropriation bill

      • Vote of Credit

        Parliament instead of Authorising a specific demand on Vote on Demand for Grant. The Parliament Gives a Blank Cheque to the Govt for Expenses

        It generally happens when the extent and nature of expense is not clear and defined

        Example: Defence Ministry cant tell that until when and what kind of costs will be incurred in a War

        Definition - It is granted for meeting an unexpected demands upon the Resources of India

        Nature of Demand and Magnitude is Indefinite in Character

      • Exceptional Grant

        It has never been utilised everywhere, there is a service for which govt needs money, the head of this service is something on which the government has never ever used money in past

        Example: Alien Has been Caught and Govt Needs Money for setting up a laboratory for the Alien

        Definition: To Make an Exceptional Grant which forms no part of current service of financial year

    • Type of Fund in the Parliament

      • Art 266: CFI - Consolidated Fund of India and Public Accounts of India & of the States

        Consolidate Fund & Public Accounts of India → Constitutional Fund

        1. Source of Fund is Constitution Itself
        2. Art 266(1) - CFI for Union and States
        3. Art 266(2) - PAI of India and States

        | CFI | PAI | | --- | --- | | Consolidated Fund of India | Public Accounts of India | | Whatever Revenue Govt makes, it is credited into CFI | Govt is the Custodian of this Fund | | They Taxable and Non Taxable Sources (Natural Resources, Fines, Profits on Investments etc) | Sources Are - Pension Funds, Small Saving Account in Post Office, Other Govt Saving Places | | Govt is NOT authorised to take money out by itself | As & When People Require, Govt does not need approval of the Parliament |

      • Art 267: Contingency Fund of India - Statutory Fund

        1. Contingency → Unforeseen and Unexpected Circumstances

        2. Art 267 → It is a Statutory Fund, Article in the Constitution Says that Parliament shall create a contingency fund

        3. Act is → Contingency Fund of India Act, 1951

        4. Source of Money → Money will be taken from CFI of India as and when budget is made

          when the Corpus of Contingency Fund is Finished, A supplementary grant is taken for it

        5. It Does not need approval of Parliament

        6. It can be done by an executive order of President of India

        7. Here President of India has already made the Finance Minster of India, Its Overseer. Hence, he/she can take out the money as and when needed

    • Legislative Council

      • Important Details of Legislative Council

        1. Art 169 provides for Legislative Council

        2. Final Authority to tell decide about it is that of Parliament of India

        3. Parliament can either create or deny the creation of Legislative Council

        4. Parliament Cannot do this action in Suo Moto Cognisance

        5. Resolution for LC in States has to be passed with Special Majority in the Parliament

        6. State LA has to first pass a resolution and then parliament can create and abolish LC for the states

        7. Remember Two Things

          1. Parliament Cannot Do it Suo Moto
          2. Resolution of State Leg Ass is not Binding upon the Parliament

        8. LC not existing in the states or it existed and then it has been abolished will make a change in the constitution of India i.e in Art 168

          So does the Parliament has to follow the process of a Constitutional Amendment ?

          No Constitutional Amendment is needed to add the name of state to the Art 168

          It can be done by an act of parliament requiring Simple Majority

          This Exception has been given in the Constitution itself

      • Summary of Adding or Abolishing a Legislative Council

      • Composition of Legislative Council

        • Total How Many Members can be there in a Legislative Council ?

          1. There is no fixed Exact Number for the Legislative Council

            1. Not More than 1/3rd Members of Leg Ass of State and Not Less than 40

          2. Example State X has 180 MLA’s

            Strength Can Range Between → 40 - 60

          3. Example State X has 100 MLA’s

            Here, It will be Exactly 40

        People who can vote in Legislative Council Election & its Composition ?

        • Summary

        • Detailed - Here People of Leg Council are ELECTED BY, not ELECTED AMONGST

          Electorates → Who Have the Right to Vote

          1/3rd Members Elected by Electorates

          1. Members of Municipalities, District Board & Other Local Authorities
          2. Members of Legislative Assembly

          1/12th Members Elected by Electorates

          1. Graduates (3 Years in Possession of Degree)

            Each Election will have one Constituency Graduates for Each Election, in Next Election Another Constituency will be given the chance

          2. Teacher Not Less than Secondary Level (Teachers from Class 9th)

          Nomination by Governor

          1. 1/6th Members to be Nominated by the Governor of state from the fields of (Art, Literature, Science, Social Science, Cooperatives)

    • CAG - Comptroller & Auditor General

      • Ambedkar's View on CAG

        Ambedkar Said that for him CAG is Guardian of Public Purse and for Ambedkar Single Most Important Constitutionally Important Functionary is CAG

      • CAG Submits the Report to President

      • CAG Helps them In

        Parliament is a very large body, hence it is not possible for everyone to do every work

        Hence DRSC Come into Play

        Committee Function Throughout the Year but Parliament Meets only when the Parliament is in Session

        • CAG Audits PSU as well

          Ex PSU : SAIL, HALE,

          PSU have Internal Offers of Themselves

          CAG is Outer Reporter

    • Parliamentary Committee

      What is the Need for Parliamentary Committees

      • Functions of Parliament & Thus of DRSC Are :
        1. They have to Make Govt Accountable
        2. To Pass the Bills and Make Laws
        3. Quasi Judicial Functions
          1. Removal of President
          2. Punishment for Breach of Parliamentary Privileges
        4. Budget Making
        5. CAG Reports are Scrutinised by the Parliamentary Committee → Financial Accountability

      Types of Parliamentary Committees

      Standing or Permanent Committees

      • Financial Committees (3)

        They help the Govt in Ensuring the Financial Accountability of Government

        | Theme | Public Accounts Committee | Committee on Public Sector Undertaking | Estimate Committee | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | Composition | 22 Members 15 - LS 7 - RS Rep from Both Houses | 22 Members 15 - LS 7 - RS Rep from Both Houses | 30 Members 30 - LS No Rep from RS | | Chairperson is Appointed by | Appointed by Lok Sabha Speaker

        As a Convention Chairperson of Public Accounts Committee is from Opposition | Appointed by Lok Sabha Speaker

        As a Convention Chairperson of Public Accounts Committee is from Opposition | Appointed by Lok Sabha Speaker

        As a Convention Chairperson is from Ruling Party | | Term | 1 Year Generally, They are Re-Elected in Parliament. Its a Convention in Parliament | | Who Can be a Member | No Minister can be a Member of this Committee

        Only a Pvt Mem can be Member | No Minister can be a Member of this Committee
